provided that
n^{2}\rho_{0}\gg\mu rn\log n
. Here,
T^{\star}

denotes the tangent space of the set of rank-
r
matrices at the
point
\bm{L}^{\star}=\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}
.

Proof.

See

[
CR09
, Theorem 4.1]
∎

In fact, the bound (
B.2
) uncovers certain
near-isometry of the operator
\rho_{0}^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}(\cdot)

when restricted to the tangent space
T^{\star}
. This property is
formalized in the following fact.

Fact 1
.

Suppose that
\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}-\rho_{0}^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\|\leq 1/2
.
Then one has

\frac{1}{2}\left\|\bm{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{3}{2}\left\|\bm{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},\qquad\text{for all }\bm{H}\in T^{\star}.

Proof.

The proof has actually been documented in
the literature. For completeness, we present the proof for the lower
bound here; the upper bound follows from a very similar argument.
For any
\bm{H}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right),\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\rangle=\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right),\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\rho_{0}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\rho_{0}\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right),\left(\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}-\rho_{0}^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\right)\left(\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\rho_{0}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\rho_{0}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}-\rho_{0}^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\right\|\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\rho_{0}}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Here, the penultimate inequality relies on the elementary fact that

\langle\bm{A},\bm{B}\rangle\leq\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
,
and the last step follows from the assumption
\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}-\rho_{0}^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\|\leq 1/2
.
∎

The following corollary is an immediate consequence of Lemma
3

and Fact
1
.

Corollary 3
.

Suppose that
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\leq 1/12

and that
n^{2}p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\gg\mu rn\log n
. Then with probability
at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, we have

\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\|^{2}\leq p/8.

Proof.

Recall the auxiliary index set
\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}

introduced in Appendix
A
. Since

\Omega^{\star}\subseteq\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}
, we have for any
\bm{H}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}

\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{3p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{3p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}{2}\left\|\bm{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Here, the second inequality arises from Lemma
3

and Fact
1
(by taking
\Omega_{0}=\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}

and
\rho_{0}=p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}
). The proof is complete by recognizing
the assumption
\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\leq 1/12
. ∎

As it turns out, the near-isometry property of
\rho_{0}^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}(\cdot)

can be strengthened to a uniform version (uniform over a large collection
of tangent spaces), as shown in the lemma below.

Lemma 4
.

Suppose that
each
(i,j)
is included in
\Omega_{0}\subseteq[n]\times[n]
independently
with probability
\rho_{0}
, and that
n^{2}\rho_{0}\gg\mu rn\log n
.
Then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\frac{1}{32\kappa}\left\|\bm{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq 40\kappa\left\|\bm{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},\qquad\text{for all }\bm{H}\in T

holds simultaneously for all
(\bm{X},\bm{Y})
obeying

\max\left\{\left\|\bm{X}-\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty},\left\|\bm{Y}-\bm{Y}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\right\}\leq\frac{c}{\kappa\sqrt{n}}\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|.

Here,
c>0
is some sufficiently small constant, and
T
denotes
the tangent space of the set of rank-
r
matrices at the point
\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}
.

Proof.

See
Appendix
B.2
.∎

In the end, we recall a useful lemma which relates the operator norm
to the
\ell_{2,\infty}
norm of a matrix.

Lemma 5
.

Suppose that each

(i,j)
is included in
\Omega_{0}\subseteq[n]\times[n]
independently
with probability
\rho_{0}
, and that
n^{2}\rho_{0}\gg n\log n
.
Then there exists some absolute constant
C>0
such that with probability
at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\left(\bm{A}\bm{B}^{\top}\right)-\rho_{0}\bm{A}\bm{B}^{\top}\right\|\leq C\sqrt{n\rho_{0}}\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{2,\infty}

holds simultaneously for all
\bm{A}
and
\bm{B}
.

Proof.

See

[
CLL20
, Lemmas 4.2 and 4.3]
.∎

B.2
Proof of Lemma
4

The lower bound has been established in
[
CCF+20
, Lemma 7]
,
and hence we focus on the upper bound. We start by expressing
\bm{H}\in T

as
\bm{H}=\bm{X}\bm{A}^{\top}+\bm{B}\bm{Y}^{\top}
, where
\bm{A},\bm{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}

are chosen to be

\displaystyle\left(\bm{A},\bm{B}\right)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\underset{(\tilde{\bm{A}},\tilde{\bm{B}}):\,\bm{H}=\bm{X}\tilde{\bm{A}}^{\top}+\tilde{\bm{B}}\bm{Y}^{\top}}{\arg\min}\Big\{\|\tilde{\bm{A}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}/2+\|\tilde{\bm{B}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}/2\Big\}.

The optimality condition of
(\bm{A},\bm{B})
requires

\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{B}=\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{Y};

(B.3)

see
[
CCF+20
, Section C.3.1]
for the justification of
this identity. The proof then consists of two steps:

1.

Showing that
\|\bm{H}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
is bounded from below,
namely,

\left\|\bm{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq\frac{49}{100}\sigma_{\min}\left(\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

To see this, we can invoke the bound on
\alpha_{2}
stated in
[
CCF+20
, Appendix C.3.1]

to yield

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\bm{X}\bm{A}^{\top}+\bm{B}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{A}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\bm{Y}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)-\frac{1}{100}\sigma_{\min}\left(\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{\min}\left(\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)-\frac{1}{100}\sigma_{\min}\left(\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)\geq\frac{49}{100}\sigma_{\min}\left(\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

2.

Showing that
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}(\bm{H})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

is bounded from above, namely,

\frac{1}{2\rho_{0}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq 9\sigma_{\max}\left(\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

To this end, one starts with the following decomposition

\frac{1}{2\rho_{0}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\left\|\bm{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\rho_{0}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\bm{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(B.4)

Apply
[
CCF+20
, Equation (83)]
to obtain

\frac{1}{2}\left\|\bm{X}\bm{A}^{\top}+\bm{B}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq 8\sigma_{\max}\left(\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

In addition, the bound on
\alpha_{1}
stated in
[
CCF+20
, Appendix C.3.1]

tells us that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\rho_{0}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\bm{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\rho_{0}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\left(\bm{X}\bm{A}^{\top}+\bm{B}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\bm{X}\bm{A}^{\top}+\bm{B}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{32}\left(\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{A}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\bm{Y}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{25}\sigma_{\min}\left(\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(\frac{1}{32}\sigma_{\max}+\frac{1}{25}\sigma_{\min}\right)\left(\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

Substitution into (
B.4
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\rho_{0}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 8\sigma_{\max}\left(\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)+\left(\frac{1}{32}\sigma_{\max}+\frac{1}{25}\sigma_{\min}\right)\left(\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 9\sigma_{\max}\left(\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

Putting the above two bounds together, we conclude that

\frac{1}{2\rho_{0}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{0}}\left(\bm{H}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq 9\sigma_{\max}\left(\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)\leq\frac{900}{49}\kappa\cdot\frac{49}{100}\sigma_{\min}\left(\left\|\bm{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)\leq 20\kappa\left\|\bm{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

as claimed.

Appendix C
Proof of Lemma
2

With Lemma
4
in place, we
can immediately justify Lemma
2
.

To begin with, the first two parts (
3.16a
)
and (
3.16b
) are the same as
[
CCF+20
, Lemma 4]
.
Hence, it suffices to verify the last one (
3.16c
). Recall
from Appendix
A
that
\Omega^{\star}\subseteq\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}
,
where
\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}
is randomly sampled such that each

(i,j)
is included in
\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}
independently with
probability
p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}
. Applying Lemma
4

on
\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}
finishes the proof, with the proviso that

\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\asymp 1/\kappa^{2}
and
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}
.

Appendix D
Crude error bounds (Proof of Theorem
3
)

This section is devoted to establishing our crude statistical error
bounds on
\|\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{L}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

and
\|\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{S}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
. Without loss of generality, we only consider the case when
\tau=\lambda\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{np}}
. The proof works for general choices
\tau\asymp\lambda\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{np}}
with slight modification. To simplify
the notation hereafter, we denote

\displaystyle\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\coloneqq\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{L}^{\star}

\displaystyle,\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\coloneqq\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{S}^{\star},

\displaystyle\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\coloneqq(\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)+\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}})/2,

\displaystyle\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\coloneqq(\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)-\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}})/2,

which immediately imply

\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}=\bm{\Lambda}^{+}+\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right),\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}=\bm{\Lambda}^{+}-\bm{\Lambda}^{-}.

These in turn allow us to decompose
\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

as follows

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}+\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}-\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\left\langle\bm{\Lambda}^{+},\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\rangle+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-2\left\langle\bm{\Lambda}^{+},\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=2\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\left\langle\bm{\Lambda}^{+},\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\rangle.

(D.1)

Since
(\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}},\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}})
is the minimizer
of (
1.3
), it is self-evident that
\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}

must be supported on
\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}
. Then by construction,

\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}},\bm{\Lambda}^{+}
and
\bm{\Lambda}^{-}

are all necessarily supported on
\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}
, thus indicating
that

\langle\bm{\Lambda}^{+},\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}})\rangle=0.

Making use of this relation, we can continue the derivation (
D.1
)
above to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=2\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\underbrace{2\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{\eqqcolon\alpha_{1}}+\underbrace{\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{\eqqcolon\alpha_{2}}+\underbrace{\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{\eqqcolon\alpha_{3}}.

In the sequel, we shall control the three terms
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}

and
\alpha_{3}
separately.

Step 1: bounding
\alpha_{1}
.

By definition, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}

\displaystyle=2\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)+\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}+\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{M}+\bm{M}-\bm{L}^{\star}-\bm{S}^{\star}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}+\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{L}^{\star}+\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}+\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

(D.2)

where the third identity holds true since
\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}=\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}})
,
the penultimate relation is due to the elementary inequality
\|\bm{A}+\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq 2\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\|\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
,
and the last line follows since
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{L}^{\star}+\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{M})=\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{E})
.
To upper bound
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}+\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{M})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
,
we leverage the optimality of
(\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}},\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}})

w.r.t. the convex program (
1.3
) to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}+\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\lambda\left\|\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{L}^{\star}+\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\lambda\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|_{1}.

(D.3)

Recognizing again that
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{L}^{\star}+\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{M})=\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{E})
,
we can rearrange terms in (
D.3
)
to derive

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}+\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\lambda\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}\right\|_{\ast}+2\tau\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|_{1}-2\lambda\big\|\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}\big\|_{\ast}-2\tau\big\|\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}\big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{(\text{i})}{\leq}\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\lambda\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{*}+2\tau\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{(\text{ii})}{\leq}\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\lambda\sqrt{n}\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+2\tau\sqrt{|\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}|}\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\text{iii})}{\leq}\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\sqrt{2}\lambda\sqrt{n\log n}\left(\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right),

(D.4)

where
|\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}|
denotes the cardinality of
\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}
.
Here, the relation (i) results from the triangle inequality, the inequality
(ii) holds true since
\|\bm{A}\|_{*}\leq\sqrt{n}\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

for any
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
and
\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\|_{1}=\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}})\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{|\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}|}\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
,
and the last line (iii) arises from the fact that
|\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}|\leq 2n^{2}p

with high probability as well as the choice
\tau=\lambda\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{np}}
.
Combine (
D.2
) and (
D.4
)
to reach

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\sqrt{2}\lambda\sqrt{n\log n}\left(\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+4\lambda\sqrt{n\log n}\sqrt{\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}},

(D.5)

where we use the elementary inequality
a+b\leq\sqrt{2}\cdot\sqrt{a^{2}+b^{2}}
.

Step 2: bounding
\alpha_{2}
via
\alpha_{3}
.

To relate
\alpha_{2}
to
\alpha_{3}
, the following lemma plays
a crucial role, whose proof is deferred to Appendix
D.1
.

Lemma 6
.

Suppose that
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\|^{2}\leq p/8

and that
\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}-p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\|\leq 1/2
.
Then for any pair
(\bm{A},\bm{B})
of matrices, we have

\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{4}{p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left[\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(D.6)

Suppose for the moment that the assumptions of Lemma
6

hold. Taking
(\bm{A},\bm{B})
as
(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}),-\bm{\Lambda}^{-})

in Lemma
6
yields

\alpha_{2}=\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{4}{p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left[\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)-\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right)\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

By virtue of the identity

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)-\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right)

\displaystyle=\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)-\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)-\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{\Lambda}^{-})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)-\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)+\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}),

we further obtain

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)-\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)+\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{\Lambda}^{-})\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left[\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)-\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{\Lambda}^{-})\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)-\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{8}{p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\frac{8}{p}\alpha_{3}.

(D.7)

Once again, the derivation has made use of the elementary inequality

\|\bm{A}+\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq 2\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\|\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
.

Step 3: bounding
\alpha_{3}
via
\alpha_{1}
and
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{E})\|_{\mathrm{F}}
.

The following lemma proves useful in linking
\alpha_{3}
with
\alpha_{1}
,
and we postpone the proof to Appendix
D.2
.

Lemma 7
.

Assume that
n^{2}p\gg n\log n
,

\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\ll 1
and
\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}-p^{-1}(1-\rho_{\mathsf{s}})^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\|\leq 1/2
.
Further assume that there exists a dual certificate
\bm{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}

such that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left[\lambda\bm{W}+\tau\mathsf{sgn}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right)-\lambda\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}^{\star\top}\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\tau/\sqrt{n},

(D.8a)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left[\lambda\bm{W}+\tau\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle<\lambda/2,

(D.8b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{W}\right)

\displaystyle=\bm{0},

(D.8c)

\displaystyle\left\|\lambda\bm{W}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle<\tau/2,

(D.8d)

where
\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right)\coloneqq[\mathsf{sign}(S_{ij}^{\star})]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}
.
Then for any
\bm{H}_{\bm{L}},\bm{H}_{\bm{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}

satisfying
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}})+\bm{H}_{\bm{S}}=\bm{0}
,
one has

\displaystyle\lambda\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}+\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{*}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{H}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\lambda\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}\right\|_{*}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|_{1}+\frac{\lambda}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{*}+\frac{\tau}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{1}.

Again, we assume for the moment that the assumptions in Lemma
7

hold. Setting
\bm{H}_{L}=\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}})

and
\bm{H}_{\bm{S}}=-\bm{\Lambda}^{-}
in Lemma
7

gives

\displaystyle\lambda\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}+\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\geq\lambda\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|_{1}+\frac{\lambda}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)\right\|_{*}+\frac{\tau}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right)\right\|_{1}.

In addition, recalling the identities
\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}=\bm{L}^{\star}+\bm{\Lambda}^{+}+\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}})

and
\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}=\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{\Lambda}^{+}-\bm{\Lambda}^{-}
,
we can invoke the triangle inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\lambda\left\|\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\lambda\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}+\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\bm{\Lambda}^{+}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\lambda\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}+\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right\|_{1}-\lambda\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{\ast}-\tau\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{1}.

Adding the above two inequalities and using the fact
\mathsf{support}(\bm{\Lambda}^{-})\subseteq\Omega_{\mathrm{obs}}

lead to

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)\right\|_{*}+\frac{\tau}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right)\right\|_{1}=\frac{\lambda}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)\right\|_{*}+\frac{\tau}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\lambda\left\|\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}\right\|_{1}+\lambda\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{1}-\lambda\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}\right\|_{\ast}-\tau\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{L}^{\star}+\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}+\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\lambda\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+4\lambda\sqrt{n\log n}\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

(D.9)

Here, the penultimate line results from the inequality (
D.3
)
and last line follows from the same argument in obtaining (
D.4
).

We are now ready to establish the upper bound on
\alpha_{3}
. Invoke
the elementary inequalities
\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|\bm{A}\|_{*}

and
\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|\bm{A}\|_{1}
for any
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}

to show that

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)\right\|_{*}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right)\right\|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{16}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{16}{\tau^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{\lambda}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right)\right\|_{*}+\frac{\tau}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{-}\right)\right\|_{1}\right)^{2}.

This combined with (
D.9
) allows us to obtain

\alpha_{3}\leq\left(\frac{16}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{16}{\tau^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+4\lambda\sqrt{n}\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right)^{2}\leq\left(\frac{32}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{32}{\tau^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{4}+16\lambda^{2}n\log n\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right),

where we have used the elementary inequality
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
.
Recalling that
\tau=\lambda/\sqrt{np/\log n}
and that
np\geq 1
,
we arrive at

\alpha_{3}\leq\frac{64np}{\lambda^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{4}+16\lambda^{2}n\log n\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)=\frac{16np}{\lambda^{2}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{4}+2^{9}n^{2}p\alpha_{1}\log n,

(D.10)

where we have identified
2\|\bm{\Lambda}^{+}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

with
\alpha_{1}
.

Step 4: putting the above bounds on
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}

together.

Taking the preceding bounds on
\alpha_{1}
,
\alpha_{2}
and
\alpha_{3}

collectively yields

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\leq}\alpha_{1}+\left(1+\frac{8}{p}\right)\alpha_{3}\overset{(\text{ii})}{\leq}\alpha_{1}+\frac{16}{p}\alpha_{3}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{\leq}\left(2^{13}n^{2}\log n+1\right)\alpha_{1}+\frac{2^{8}n}{\lambda^{2}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{4}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iv})}{\leq}\left(2^{13}n^{2}\log n+1\right)\left[2\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+4\lambda\sqrt{n\log n}\sqrt{\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\right]+\frac{2^{8}n}{\lambda^{2}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{4}.

Here, the first inequality (i) comes from (
D.7
),
the second inequality (ii) follows from the fact
1\leq 8/p
, the third relation
(iii) is a consequence of (
D.10
), and the last
line (iv) results from (
D.5
). Note that this forms
a quadratic inequality in
\sqrt{\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}
.
Solving the inequality yields the claimed bound

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{2}n^{5}\log^{3}n+n^{2}\log n\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{n}{\lambda^{2}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{4}.

Further, the elementary inequality
a^{2}+b^{2}\geq 2ab
yields

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}n^{5}\log^{3}n+\frac{n}{\lambda^{2}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{4}

\displaystyle\geq 2n^{3}\log^{3/2}n\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq n^{2}\log n\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

leading to the simplified bound

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{2}n^{5}\log^{3}n+\frac{n}{\lambda^{2}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{4}.

Step 5: checking the conditions in Lemmas
6

and
7
.

We are left with proving that the conditions in Lemmas
6

and
7
hold with high probability. In
view of Lemma
3
and Corollary
3
,
the conditions
\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}-p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\|\leq 1/2

and
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\|^{2}\leq p/8

hold with high probability, provided that
n^{2}p\gg\mu rn\log n

and
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq 1/12
. In addition, Lemma
3

ensures that
\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}-p^{-1}(1-\rho_{\mathsf{s}})^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\|\leq 1/2

holds with high probability, with the proviso that
n^{2}p(1-\rho_{\mathsf{s}})\gg\mu rn\log n
,
which holds true under the assumptions
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq 1/12

and
n^{2}p\gg\mu rn\log n
. Last but not least, the existence of
the dual certificate
\bm{W}
obeying (
D.8
) is guaranteed
with high probability according to
[
CJSC13
, Section III.D]
,
under the conditions
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\ll 1
and
n^{2}p\gg\mu^{2}r^{2}n\log^{6}n
.
10
10

10

Note that
[
CJSC13
, Section III.D]
requires
n^{2}p\gg\max\{\mu,\mu_{2}\}rn\log^{6}n

under an additional incoherence condition
\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}^{\star\top}\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{\mu_{2}r/n^{2}}
.
While we do not impose this extra condition, it is easily seen that

\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}^{\star\top}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\bm{U}^{\star}\|_{2,\infty}\|\bm{V}^{\star}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\mu r/n

and hence
\mu_{2}\leq\mu^{2}r
.

D.1
Proof of Lemma
6

Expand
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}(\bm{A})+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}(\bm{B}))\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left[\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right),\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\frac{p}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right),\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right\rangle.

Here, the equality uses the fact
\Omega^{\star}\subseteq\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}
,
and the inequality holds because of the assumption
\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}-p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\|\leq 1/2

and Fact
1
. Use
\Omega^{\star}\subseteq\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}

once again to obtain

\displaystyle 2\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right),\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=2\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right),\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq-2\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\right\|\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq-2\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\right\|^{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Here, the last relation arises from the elementary inequality
ab\leq(a^{2}+b^{2})/2

and the fact that
\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\right\|\leq 1
.
Combine the above two inequalities to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left[\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{p}{2}-2\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\right\|^{2}\right)\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{p}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{p}{4}\left(\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{A}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

as claimed, where we have used the assumption
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\|^{2}\leq p/8

in the middle line and the fact
1/2\geq p/4
in the last inequality.

D.2
Proof of Lemma
7

In view of the convexity of the nuclear norm
\|\cdot\|_{\ast}
,
one has

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}+\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\ast}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}\right\|_{\ast}+\left\langle\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}^{\star\top}+\bm{G}_{1},\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right\rangle=\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}\right\|_{\ast}+\left\langle\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}^{\star\top},\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right\rangle+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}.

Here,
\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}^{\star\top}+\bm{G}_{1}
is a sub-gradient
of
\|\cdot\|_{*}
at
\bm{L}^{\star}
. The last identity holds
by choosing
\bm{G}_{1}
such that
\langle\bm{G}_{1},\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\rangle=\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}})\|_{\ast}
.
Similarly, using the assumption
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}})+\bm{H}_{\bm{S}}=\bm{0}

and the convexity of the
\ell_{1}
norm
\|\cdot\|_{1}
, we can
obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{H}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}-\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|_{1}-\left\langle\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right)+\bm{G}_{2},\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{=}\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|_{1}-\left\langle\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right),\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\rangle+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(ii)}}{=}\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|_{1}-\left\langle\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right),\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right\rangle+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{1},

where
\mathsf{sign}(\bm{S}^{\star})\coloneqq[\mathsf{sign}(S_{ij}^{\star})]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}
,
and
\mathsf{sign}(\bm{S}^{\star})+\bm{G}_{2}
is a sub-gradient
of
\|\cdot\|_{1}
at
\bm{S}^{\star}
. The first equality (i) holds
by choosing
\bm{G}_{2}
such that
-\langle\bm{G}_{2},\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}})\rangle=\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}})\|_{1}
,
and the last relation (ii) arises since
\mathsf{sign}(\bm{S}^{\star})

is supported on
\Omega^{\star}\subseteq\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}
. Combine
the above two bounds to deduce that

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle\coloneqq\lambda\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}+\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{H}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{1}-\lambda\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}\right\|_{*}-\tau\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\lambda\left\langle\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}^{\star\top},\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right\rangle+\lambda\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}-\tau\left\langle\mathsf{sgn}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right),\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right\rangle+\tau\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\lambda\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}^{\star\top}-\tau\mathsf{sgn}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right),\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right\rangle+\lambda\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left\langle\lambda\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}^{\star\top}-\tau\mathsf{sgn}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right)-\lambda\bm{W},\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right\rangle}_{\eqqcolon\theta_{1}}+\underbrace{\left\langle\lambda\bm{W},\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right\rangle}_{\eqqcolon\theta_{2}}+\lambda\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{1},

(D.11)

where
\bm{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is the dual certificate stated
in Lemma
7
.

In what follows, we shall lower bound the right-hand side of (
D.11
).
To begin with, for
\theta_{1}
we have

\displaystyle\theta_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left[\lambda\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}^{\star\top}-\tau\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right)-\lambda\bm{W}\right],\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\rangle+\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left[\lambda\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}^{\star\top}-\tau\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right)-\lambda\bm{W}\right],\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left[\lambda\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}^{\star\top}-\tau\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right)-\lambda\bm{W}\right],\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left[\tau\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right)+\lambda\bm{W}\right],\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left[\lambda\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}^{\star\top}-\tau\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right)-\lambda\bm{W}\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left[\tau\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right)+\lambda\bm{W}\right]\right\|\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\tau}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\frac{\lambda}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}.

Here, the penultimate line uses the fact
\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}^{\star\top}\in T^{\star}

and the elementary inequalities
|\langle\bm{A},\bm{B}\rangle|\leq\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

and
|\langle\bm{A},\bm{B}\rangle|\leq\|\bm{A}\|\|\bm{B}\|_{\ast}
,
whereas the last inequality relies on the properties of the dual certificate

\bm{W}
, namely, (
D.8a
) and (
D.8b
). Moving
on to
\theta_{2}
, one has

\displaystyle\theta_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\lambda\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{W}\right),\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right\rangle+\left\langle\lambda\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{W}\right),\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{\text{}}{=}\left\langle\lambda\bm{W},\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\rangle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\geq}-\left\|\lambda\bm{W}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{1}\overset{\text{(ii)}}{\geq}-\frac{\tau}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{1}.

Here, the second identity uses the assumption (
D.8c
), the
first inequality (i) uses the elementary inequality
|\langle\bm{A},\bm{B}\rangle|\leq\|\bm{A}\|_{\infty}\|\bm{B}\|_{1}
,
and the last relation (ii) holds because of the assumption (
D.8d
).
Substituting the above two bounds back into (
D.11
)
gives

\Delta\geq-\frac{\tau}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}+\frac{\tau}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{1}.

(D.12)

Continuing the lower bound, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\geq}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(ii)}}{\geq}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

where (i) holds because
\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{1}}\geq\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

for any matrix
\bm{A}
, and (ii) arises from the triangle inequality.
Putting the above relation and (
D.12
) together
results in

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\tau}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left(\frac{\lambda}{2}-\frac{\tau}{4}\right)\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}+\frac{\tau}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\frac{\tau}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\tau}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\frac{\tau}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\frac{\lambda}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}+\frac{\tau}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{1},

(D.13)

where the last line holds since
\lambda=\tau\sqrt{np/\log n}\geq\tau

(as long as
np\geq\log n
). Everything then boils down to lower
bounding
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}})\|_{\mathrm{F}}
.
To this end, one can use the assumption
\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}-p^{-1}(1-\rho_{\mathsf{s}})^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\|\leq 1/2

and Fact
1
to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}p\left(1-\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\right)\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(D.14)

Take (
D.13
) and (
D.14
) collectively
to yield

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{\tau}{4}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}p\left(1-\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\right)}-\frac{\tau}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\frac{\lambda}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}+\frac{\tau}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\lambda}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\star\perp}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}+\frac{\tau}{4}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{1},

where the last relation is guaranteed by
np\gg 1
and
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\ll 1
.
Recognizing that
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}(\bm{H}_{\bm{L}})=-\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}(\bm{H}_{\bm{S}})

finishes the proof.

Appendix E
Equivalence between convex and nonconvex solutions (Proof of Theorem

4
)

The goal of this section is to establish the intimate connection between
the convex and nonconvex solutions (cf. Theorem
4
).
Before continuing, we remind the readers of the following notations:

•

\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}=\bm{U}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{V}^{\top}
: the rank-
r

singular value decomposition of
\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}
;

•

T
: the tangent space of the set of rank-
r
matrices at the estimate

\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}
.

In addition, we define

\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\coloneqq\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top},\qquad\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\coloneqq\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{S},

(E.1)

and denote the support of
\bm{S}
by

\Omega\coloneqq\{(i,j)\,|\,S_{ij}\neq 0\}.

(E.2)

E.1
Preliminary facts

We begin with two useful lemmas which demonstrate that the point
(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top},\bm{S})

described in Theorem
4
satisfies approximate
optimality conditions w.r.t. the convex program (
1.3
).

Lemma 8
.

Instate the assumptions in Theorem
4
.
The triple
(\bm{X},\bm{Y},\bm{S})
as stated in Theorem
4

satisfies

\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{S}-\bm{M}\right)=-\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\top}+\bm{R}_{1}

(E.3)

for some matrix
\bm{R}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
obeying

\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{R}_{1}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\frac{\kappa p\left\|\nabla f\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y};\bm{S}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\lambda\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{19}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}\left(\bm{R}_{1}\right)\right\|\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(E.4)

Proof.

The proof can be straightforwardly adapted
from
[
CCF+20
, Claim 2]
by replacing
\bm{E}
therein
with
\bm{E}+\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{S}
. We omit it for the sake of brevity.
∎

Lemma 9
.

The point
(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top},\bm{S})

as stated in Theorem
4
obeys

\frac{1}{\tau}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{S}-\bm{M}\right)=-\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}\right)+\bm{R}_{2}

(E.5)

for some matrix
\bm{R}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, where
\bm{R}_{2}

satisfies

\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{R}_{2}\right)=\bm{0}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{R}_{2})\right\|_{\infty}\leq 1

(E.6)

with
\Omega
defined in (
E.2
).

Proof.

By
definition, one has
\bm{S}=\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}[\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top})]
.
Clearly, this is equivalent to saying that
\bm{S}
is the unique
minimizer of the following convex program

\bm{S}=\underset{\widehat{\bm{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}}{\arg\min}\>\,\frac{1}{2}\Bigl\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\big(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\widehat{\bm{S}}-\bm{M}\big)\Bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\tau\big\|\widehat{\bm{S}}\big\|_{1}.

(E.7)

The claim of this lemma then follows from the optimality condition
of this convex program (
E.7
). ∎

Additionally, in view of the crude error bound (
3.8
)
and Condition
1
, the matrix
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}

(cf. (
E.1
)) obeys

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{L}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}-\bm{L}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{L}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+n\left\|\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}-\bm{L}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma n^{4}+n\tau\asymp\sigma n^{4},

(E.8)

where we use the the elementary inequality
\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq n\|\bm{A}\|_{\infty}

and the fact that
\tau\asymp\sigma\sqrt{\log n}
.

E.2
Proof of Theorem
4

We now present the proof of Theorem
4
,
which consists of three main steps:

1.

Showing that
(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top},\bm{S})
is not far from
(\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}},\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}})

over
\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}
, in the sense that
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\approx\bm{0}
;

2.

Showing that
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}
(resp.
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}
)
is extremely small outside the tangent space
T
(resp. the support

\Omega^{\star}
), and hence most of the energy of
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}

(resp.
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}
) — if it is not vanishingly small
— has to reside within
T
(resp.
\Omega^{\star}
);

3.

Showing that
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\approx\bm{0}
and
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\approx\bm{0}
,
with the assistance of the preceding two steps.

In what follows, we shall detail each of these steps.

E.2.1
Step 1: showing that
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\approx\bm{0}

Since
(\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}},\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}})
is the minimizer
of the convex program (
1.3
), we have

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}+\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\lambda\left\|\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad=\tfrac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{S}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\lambda\left\|\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{S}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\lambda\left\|\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right\|_{\ast}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}\right\|_{1}.

Here, the equality arises from the relations
\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}}=\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}

and
\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}}=\bm{S}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}
. Expanding
the squares and rearranging terms, we arrive at

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{S}-\bm{M}\right),\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right\rangle+\lambda\left\|\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right\|_{\ast}-\lambda\left\|\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\ast}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\tau\left\|\bm{S}\right\|_{1}-\tau\left\|\bm{S}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{1}.

(E.9)

In view of the convexity of the nuclear norm
\|\cdot\|_{*}
and
the
\ell_{1}
norm
\|\cdot\|_{1}
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\ast}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right\|_{\ast}+\big\langle\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\top}+\bm{W},\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\big\rangle\overset{(\text{i})}{=}\left\|\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right\|_{\ast}+\big\langle\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\top},\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\big\rangle+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}})\right\|_{\ast};

(E.10a)

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{S}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\bm{S}\right\|_{1}+\big\langle\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}\right)+\bm{G},\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\big\rangle\overset{(\text{ii})}{=}\left\|\bm{S}\right\|_{1}+\big\langle\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}\right),\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\big\rangle+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\right\|_{1}.

(E.10b)

Here,
\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\top}+\bm{W}
is a sub-gradient
of
\|\cdot\|_{*}
at
\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}
. The identity (i) holds
by choosing
\bm{W}
such that
\langle\bm{W},\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\rangle=\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}})\|_{\ast}
.
Similarly,
\mathsf{sign}(\bm{S})+\bm{G}
is a sub-gradient of
\|\cdot\|_{1}

at
\bm{S}
and one can choose
\bm{G}
obeying
\langle\bm{G},\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\rangle=\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\|_{1}

to make (ii) valid. These taken together with (
E.9
)
lead to

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\big\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{S}-\bm{M}\right),\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\big\rangle-\lambda\big\langle\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\top},\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\big\rangle-\lambda\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}

\displaystyle\quad-\tau\big\langle\mathsf{sign}\left(\bm{S}\right),\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\big\rangle-\tau\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{1}.

Recall the definitions of
\bm{R}_{1}
and
\bm{R}_{2}
from Lemmas

8
and
9
. We can then simplify the above
inequality as

\tfrac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\underbrace{-\lambda\left\langle\bm{R}_{1},\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right\rangle-\lambda\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}})\right\|_{\ast}}_{\eqqcolon\theta_{1}}\underbrace{-\tau\left\langle\bm{R}_{2},\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right\rangle-\tau\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\right\|_{1}}_{\eqqcolon\theta_{2}}.

(E.11)

In the sequel, we develop bounds on
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{2}
.

1.

With regards to
\theta_{1}
, one can further decompose it into

\displaystyle\theta_{1}

\displaystyle=-\lambda\left\langle\bm{R}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\rangle-\lambda\left\langle\bm{R}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\rangle-\lambda\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{R}_{1}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\lambda\left(1-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}\left(\bm{R}_{1}\right)\right\|\right)\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{R}_{1}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\frac{\lambda}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\ast},

(E.12)

where the middle line arises from the elementary inequalities
|\langle\bm{A},\bm{B}\rangle|\leq\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

and
|\langle\bm{A},\bm{B}\rangle|\leq\|\bm{A}\|\|\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{*}}
,
and the last inequality holds since
\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\bm{R}_{1})\|\leq 1/2

(see Lemma
8
).

2.

Similarly, one can decompose
\theta_{2}
into

\displaystyle\theta_{2}

\displaystyle=-\tau\left\langle\bm{R}_{2},\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\rangle-\tau\left\langle\bm{R}_{2},\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\rangle-\tau\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\tau\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{R}_{2}\right),\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\rangle-\tau\left(1-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{R}_{2}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\right)\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{1}\leq 0.

(E.13)

Here, the first inequality comes from the facts that
|\langle\bm{A},\bm{B}\rangle|\leq\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{\infty}}\|\bm{B}\|_{1}

and
|\langle\bm{A},\bm{B}\rangle|\leq\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
,
and the second one utilizes the facts that
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}(\bm{R}_{2})=\bm{0}

and
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{R}_{2})\|_{\infty}\leq 1

(cf. Lemma
9
).

Combining (
E.11
), (
E.12
) and (
E.13
)
yields

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}(\bm{R}_{1})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\frac{\lambda}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}})\right\|_{\ast}

(E.14)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\lambda}{n^{19}}\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\frac{\sigma\sqrt{np}}{n^{19}}\sigma n^{4}\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n^{14.5}},

where we make use of the upper bound
\|\mathcal{P}_{T}(\bm{R}_{1})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim n^{-19}

(cf. Lemma
8
), the choice
\lambda\asymp\sigma\sqrt{np}

as well as the crude error bound
\|\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\sigma n^{4}

(cf. (
E.8
)). Consequently, we have demonstrated
that
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\approx\bm{0}

in the sense that

\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\frac{\sigma}{n^{7.25}}\leq\frac{\sigma}{n^{7}}.

(E.15)

E.2.2
Step 2: showing that
\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}})\approx\bm{0}

and
\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\approx\bm{0}

We begin by demonstrating that
\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}})\approx\bm{0}
.
From the inequality (
E.14
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}})\right\|_{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}(\bm{R}_{1})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\frac{1}{2\lambda}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}(\bm{R}_{1})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{19}}\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

where the last inequality again results from the estimate
\|\mathcal{P}_{T}(\bm{R}_{1})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim n^{-19}

given in Lemma
8
. Invoking the condition
\|\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\sigma n^{4}

(cf. (
E.8
)) yields

\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}})\right\|_{\ast}\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{19}}\sigma n^{4}\lesssim\frac{\sigma}{n^{15}}\leq\frac{\sigma}{n^{14}},

(E.16)

which demonstrates that the energy of
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}
outside

T
is extremely small.

We now move on to
\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})
.
This term obeys

\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})=\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}),

where the relation holds since
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}
is supported
on
\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}
. To facilitate the analysis of
\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega^{\star}
,
we introduce another index subset

\Omega_{1}\coloneqq\left\{(i,j)\in\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\,:\,|(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})_{ij}|\leq\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\|_{\infty}\right\}.

(E.17)

The usefulness of
\Omega_{1}
can be seen through the following
claim, whose claim is postponed to the end of this section.

Claim
1
.

Under Condition
1
,
we have

\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega^{\star}\subseteq\Omega_{1}.

An immediate consequence of Claim
1
is that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\setminus\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{1}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq n\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{1}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\leq n\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq n\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{\sigma}{n^{6}},

(E.18)

which justifies our assertion that the energy of
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}

outside
\Omega^{\star}
is extremely small. Here, the last inequality
arises from (
E.15
).

E.2.3
Step 3: controlling the size of
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}
(and hence
that of
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}
)

In view of (
E.15
) and the triangle
inequality, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma}{n^{7}}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega^{\star})^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\mathcal{P}_{T}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\frac{\sigma}{n^{6}}-\frac{\sigma}{n^{14}},

(E.19)

where the last step follows from (
E.16
) and (
E.18
).
By Condition
2
, we have

\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\geq\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mathrm{inj}}}{\kappa}p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mathrm{inj}}}{\kappa}p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

given that
\mathcal{P}_{T}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}})\in T
. The latter
inequality combined with (
E.15
) and
(
E.16
) further gives

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma}{n^{7}}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mathrm{inj}}}{\kappa}p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\frac{\sigma}{n^{14}}.

Substituting the above bounds into (
E.19
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma}{n^{7}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mathrm{inj}}}{\kappa}p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mathrm{inj}}}{\kappa}p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\frac{\sigma}{n^{7}}-\frac{\sigma}{n^{6}}-\frac{2\sigma}{n^{14}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mathrm{inj}}}{\kappa}p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\frac{2\sigma}{n^{6}},

which further yields

\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\frac{\sigma}{n^{6}}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa}{p}}\leq\frac{\sigma}{n^{5}},

provided that
n^{2}p\gg\kappa
. This combined with (
E.16
)
allows one to control the size of
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}
:

\displaystyle\bigl\|\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\frac{\sigma}{n^{5}}.

In view of (
E.15
) and the fact that

\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}
is supported on
\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}
,
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\frac{\sigma}{n^{5}},

thus concluding the proof.

E.2.4
Proof of Claim
1

We first recall the facts that

\bm{S}=\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left[\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right)\right]\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bm{S}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}=\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left[\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}-\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\right)\right],

where the second identity follows since
(\bm{L}_{\mathsf{cvx}},\bm{S}_{\mathsf{cvx}})=(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}},\bm{S}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})

is the optimizer of the convex program (
1.3
). These allow
us to write

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left[\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\big(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}-\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}\big)-\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left[\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}-(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\right]-\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right)\right].

(E.20)

This characterization of
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}
turns out to be crucial
when establishing the inclusion
\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega^{\star}\subseteq\Omega_{1}
.
Towards this end, we need to introduce another index subset

\Omega_{2}\coloneqq\left\{(i,j)\in\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\,:\,\tau-\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\|_{\infty}\leq\big|\big(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\big)_{ij}\big|\leq\tau\right\}.

As it turns out, the sets
\Omega,\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
obey
the following three conditions

\Omega_{2}\cap\Omega=\varnothing,\qquad\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega\subseteq\Omega_{1}\cup\Omega_{2},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Omega\cup\Omega_{2}\subseteq\Omega^{\star},

which immediately lead to

\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega^{\star}\,\overset{(\text{i})}{\subseteq}\,\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash(\Omega\cup\Omega_{2})\,\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{=}\,(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega)\backslash\Omega_{2}\,\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{\subseteq}\,(\Omega_{1}\cup\Omega_{2})\backslash\Omega_{2}\,\subseteq\,\Omega_{1}.

Here, (i) follows since
\Omega\cup\Omega_{2}\subseteq\Omega^{\star}
,
(ii) holds true since
\Omega_{2}\cap\Omega=\varnothing
, and (iii)
results from the condition
\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega\subseteq\Omega_{1}\cup\Omega_{2}
.
It then boils down to proving each of the above three conditions.

1.

The first one
\Omega_{2}\cap\Omega=\varnothing
is straightforward
to establish. Note that for any
(i,j)\in\Omega_{2}
, one must have

\big|\big(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\big)_{ij}\big|\leq\tau
and
hence
\big[\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top})\big]_{ij}=0
,
which means that
(i,j)\notin\Omega
. This proves the relation
\Omega_{2}\cap\Omega=\varnothing
.

2.

Moving on to the second one
\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega\subseteq\Omega_{1}\cup\Omega_{2}
,
we prove this via contradiction. Suppose that this inclusion is false,
i.e. there exits an index
(i,j)\in\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega

such that

|(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})_{ij}|>\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\|_{\infty}\quad\text{and}\quad|(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top})_{ij}|<\tau-\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\|_{\infty}.

(E.21)

Here, we have taken into account the fact that

|(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top})_{ij}|\leq\tau,\qquad\text{for any }(i,j)\in\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega.

To reach contradiction, we find it convenient to state the following
simple fact.

Fact 2
.

Suppose that
|a|\leq\tau

and that
\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(a+b)\neq 0
. Then

\big|\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(a+b)\big|\leq|b|+|a|-\tau.

Proof.

Given that
\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(a+b)\neq 0
,
one necessarily has
|a+b|>\tau
. Without loss of generality, assume
that
a+b>0
, which gives

\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(a+b)=a+b-\tau>0.

This together with the fact
\tau\geq|a|
yields
|\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(a+b)|=a+b-\tau\leq|b|+|a|-\tau
.
∎

With this fact in mind, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\big|\big(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\big)_{ij}\big|

\displaystyle=\left|\left\{\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}-(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\right]-\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right)\right\}_{ij}\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{=}\left|\left\{\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}-(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\right]\right\}_{ij}\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}\left|\left[\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}-\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right]_{ij}\right|+\big|\left(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right)_{ij}\big|-\tau

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{<}\big|\big(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\big)_{ij}\big|+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\right\|_{\infty}-\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\big|\big(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\big)_{ij}\big|,

(E.22)

where (i) holds true since
\big[\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top})\big]_{ij}=0

for any
(i,j)\in\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega
, (ii) follows
from Fact
2
(by taking
a=(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top})_{ij}

and
b=\left[\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}-\left(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}}\right)\right]_{ij}
),
and (iii) is a consequence of (
E.21
) as well as the
triangle inequality. The inequality (
E.22
),
however, is clearly impossible. This establishes that
\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega\subseteq\Omega_{1}\cup\Omega_{2}
.

3.

We are left with the last one
\Omega\cup\Omega_{2}\subseteq\Omega^{\star}
,
which is equivalent to saying
\Omega\subseteq\Omega^{\star}
and

\Omega_{2}\subseteq\Omega^{\star}
. First, for any
(i,j)\in\Omega
,
one has

\displaystyle\big|S_{ij}\big|>0\quad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad(i,j)\in\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\quad\text{and}\quad\bigl|\left(\bm{L}^{\star}+\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right)_{ij}\bigr|>\tau

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad(i,j)\in\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\quad\text{and}\quad\bigl|S_{ij}^{\star}\bigr|>\tau-\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right\|_{\infty}-\left\|\bm{E}\right\|_{\infty}>0.

Here, the last step comes from the triangle inequality and Condition
1
.
This reveals that
\Omega\subseteq\Omega^{\star}
. Similarly, for
any
(i,j)\in\Omega_{2}
we have

\displaystyle\tau-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\big|\big(\bm{M}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\big)_{ij}\big|

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\quad\quad

\displaystyle\tau-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\big|\big(\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{L}^{\star}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{E}\big)_{ij}\big|

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad\quad

\displaystyle\bigl|S_{ij}^{\star}\bigr|\geq\tau-\left\|\bm{L}^{\star}-\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}\right\|_{\infty}-\left\|\bm{E}\right\|_{\infty}-\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{L}}+\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{S}})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\geq\frac{\tau}{2}-\frac{\sigma}{n^{7}}>0,

where we have used Condition
1
,
the bound (
E.15
), and the fact that

\tau\gg\sigma
. This demonstrates that
\Omega_{2}\subseteq\Omega^{\star}
.
We have therefore justified that
\Omega\cup\Omega_{2}\subseteq\Omega^{\star}
.

Appendix F
Analysis of the nonconvex procedure (Proof of Theorem
5
)

This section is devoted to establishing Theorem
5
.
For notational convenience, we introduce
