\bm{F}^{t}\coloneqq\left[\bm{X}^{t\top},\bm{Y}^{t\top}\right]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{2n\times r}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bm{F}^{\star}\coloneqq\left[\bm{X}^{\star\top},\bm{Y}^{\star\top}\right]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{2n\times r}.

(F.1)

These allow us to express succinctly the rotation matrix
\bm{H}^{t}

defined in (
3.10
) as

\bm{H}^{t}=\arg\min_{\bm{R}\in\mathcal{O}^{r\times r}}\left\|\bm{F}^{t}\bm{R}-\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

(F.2)

With the definitions of
\bm{F}^{t}
and
\bm{H}^{t}
in mind, it
suffices to justify that: for all
0\leq t\leq t_{0}=n^{47}
, the
following hypotheses

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{F}^{t}\bm{H}^{t}-\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{F}}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

(F.3a)

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{F}^{t}\bm{H}^{t}-\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{op}}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|,

(F.3b)

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{F}^{t}\bm{H}^{t}-\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\infty}\kappa\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty},

(F.3c)

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{X}^{t\top}\bm{X}^{t}-\bm{Y}^{t\top}\bm{Y}^{t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{B}}\kappa\eta\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\sqrt{r}\sigma_{\max}^{2},

(F.3d)

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{S}^{t}-\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{S}}\sigma\sqrt{np}

(F.3e)

hold for some universal constants
C_{\mathrm{F}}
,

C_{\mathrm{op}}
,
C_{\infty}
,
C_{\mathrm{B}}
,
C_{\mathrm{S}}>0
,
and, in addition,

F\left(\bm{X}^{t},\bm{Y}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)\leq F\left(\bm{X}^{t-1},\bm{Y}^{t-1};\bm{S}^{t-1}\right)-\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|\nabla f\left(\bm{X}^{t-1},\bm{Y}^{t-1};\bm{S}^{t-1}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(F.4)

holds for all
1\leq t\leq t_{0}=n^{47}
.

Clearly, the bounds (
3.11a
), (
3.11b
),
(
3.11c
), and (
3.11d
) in Theorem
5

follow immediately from (
F.3a
), (
F.3b
),
(
F.3c
), and (
F.3e
),
respectively. It remains to justify the small gradient bound (
3.12
)
on the basis of (
F.3
) and (
F.4
),
which is exactly the content of the following lemma.

Lemma 10

(
Small gradient
)
.

Set

\lambda=C_{\lambda}\sigma\sqrt{np\log n}
for some large constant

C_{\lambda}>0
. Suppose that
n^{2}p\gg\kappa^{3}\mu rn\log^{2}n

and that the noise satisfies
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\ll\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa^{4}\mu r\log n}}
.
Take
\eta\asymp 1/(n\kappa^{3}\sigma_{\max})
. If the iterates satisfy
(
F.3
) for all
0\leq t\leq t_{0}
and
(
F.4
) for all
1\leq t\leq t_{0}
, then with
probability at least
1-O(n^{-50})
, one has

\min_{0\leq t<t_{0}}\left\|\nabla f\left(\bm{X}^{t},\bm{Y}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{1}{n^{20}}\frac{\lambda}{p}\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}.

Proof.

See Appendix
F.3
.∎

The remainder of this section is thus dedicated to showing that (
F.3
)
and (
F.4
) hold for
\{(\bm{F}^{t},\bm{S}^{t})\}_{0\leq t\leq t_{0}}
,
which we accomplish via mathematical induction. Throughout this section,
we let
\bm{X}_{l,\cdot}
denote the
l
th row of a matrix
\bm{X}
.

F.1
Leave-one-out analysis

The above hypotheses (
F.3
) require, among
other things, sharp control of the
\ell_{2,\infty}
estimation errors,
which calls for fine-grained statistical analyses. In order to decouple
complicated statistical dependency, we resort to the following leave-one-out
analysis framework that has been successfully applied to analyze other
nonconvex algorithms
[
ZB18
,
MWCC20
,
CLL20
,
CCFM19
,
CCF+20
,
CLPC20
,
LWC+20
,
DC20
]
.

Leave-one-out loss functions.

For each
1\leq l\leq n
, we define the following auxiliary loss
functions

\displaystyle F^{(l)}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y},\bm{S}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\coloneqq\underbrace{\frac{1}{2p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}})_{-l,\cdot}}\left(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{S}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\left(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}-\bm{L}^{\star}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2p}\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2p}\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{\eqqcolon f^{\left(l\right)}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y};\bm{S}\right)}+\frac{\tau}{p}\left\|\bm{S}\right\|_{1}.

Here,
\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}})_{-l,\cdot}}(\cdot)
(resp.
\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}(\cdot)
)
denotes orthogonal projection onto the space of matrices supported
on the index set
\{(i,j)\in\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\mid i\neq l\}

(resp.
\{(i,j)\mid i=l\}
), namely, for any matrix
\bm{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}

one has

\left[\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}})_{-l,\cdot}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right]_{ij}=\begin{cases}B_{ij},&\text{if }(i,j)\in\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\text{ and }i\neq l,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\left[\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right]_{ij}=\begin{cases}B_{ij},&\text{if }i=l,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

The above auxiliary loss function is obtained by dropping the randomness
coming from the
l
th row of
\bm{M}
, which, as we shall see shortly,
facilitates analysis in establishing the incoherence properties (
F.3c
).
Similarly, we define for each
n+1\leq l\leq 2n
that

\displaystyle F^{(l)}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y},\bm{S}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\coloneqq\underbrace{\frac{1}{2p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}})_{\cdot,-(l-n)}}\left(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{S}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\cdot,(l-n)}\left(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}-\bm{L}^{\star}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2p}\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2p}\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{\eqqcolon f^{\left(l\right)}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y};\bm{S}\right)}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}\right\|_{1},

where the projection operators
\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}})_{\cdot,-(l-n)}}(\cdot)

and
\mathcal{P}_{\cdot,(l-n)}(\cdot)
are defined such that for
any matrix
\bm{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
,

\left[\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}})_{\cdot,-(l-n)}}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right]_{ij}=\begin{cases}B_{ij},&\text{if }(i,j)\in\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\text{ and }j\neq l-n,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\left[\mathcal{P}_{\cdot,(l-n)}\left(\bm{B}\right)\right]_{ij}=\begin{cases}B_{ij},&\text{if }j=l-n,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Again, this auxiliary loss function is produced in a way that is independent
from the
(l-n)
-th column of
\bm{M}
. In the above notation,
f^{\left(l\right)}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y};\bm{S}\right)

is a function of
\bm{X}
and
\bm{Y}
with
\bm{S}
frozen.

Leave-one-out auxiliary sequences.

For each
1\leq l\leq 2n
, we construct a sequence of leave-one-out
iterates
\{\bm{F}^{t,(l)},\bm{S}^{t,(l)}\}_{t\geq 0}
via Algorithm

2
.

Algorithm 2

Construction of the
l
th leave-one-out sequences.

Initialization
:
\bm{X}^{0,(l)}=\bm{X}^{\star}
,

\bm{Y}^{0,(l)}=\bm{Y}^{\star}
,
\bm{S}^{0,(l)}=\bm{S}^{\star}
,

\bm{F}^{0,(l)}\coloneqq\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{X}^{0,(l)}\\
\bm{Y}^{0,(l)}\end{array}\right]
, and the step size
\eta>0
.

Gradient updates
:
for
t=0,1,\ldots,t_{0}-1

do

\displaystyle\bm{F}^{t+1,(l)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{X}^{t+1,(l)}\\
\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)}\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{X}^{t,(l)}-\eta\nabla_{\bm{X}}f^{(l)}(\bm{X}^{t,(l)},\bm{Y}^{t,(l)};\bm{S}^{t,(l)})\\
\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}-\eta\nabla_{\bm{Y}}f^{(l)}(\bm{X}^{t,(l)},\bm{Y}^{t,(l)};\bm{S}^{t,(l)})\end{array}\right];

\displaystyle\bm{S}^{t+1,(l)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}})_{-l,\cdot}}\left(\bm{M}-\bm{X}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)\top}\right)\right]+\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right),&\text{if }1\leq l\leq n,\\
\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}})_{\cdot,-(l-n)}}\left(\bm{M}-\bm{X}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)\top}\right)\right]+\mathcal{P}_{\cdot,(l-n)}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right),&\text{if }n+1\leq l\leq 2n.\end{cases}

(F.5e)

Properties of leave-one-out sequences.

There are several features of the leave-one-out sequences that prove
useful for our statistical analysis: (1) for the
l
th leave-one-out
sequence, one can exploit the statistical independence to control
the estimation error of
\bm{F}^{t,(l)}
in the
l
th row; (2) the
leave-one-out sequences and the original sequence
(\bm{F}^{t},\bm{S}^{t})

are exceedingly close (since we have only discarded a small amount
of information). These properties taken collectively allow us to control
the estimation error of
\bm{F}^{t}
in each row. To formalize these
features, we make an additional set of induction hypotheses

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{F}^{t}\bm{H}^{t}-\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty},

(F.6a)

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq 2n}\big\|\big(\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{H}^{t,(l)}-\bm{F}^{\star}\big)_{l,\cdot}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\kappa\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty},

(F.6b)

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq n}\big\|\mathcal{P}_{-l,\cdot}\big(\bm{S}^{t}-\bm{S}^{t,(l)}\big)\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{n\log n}\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty},

(F.6c)

\displaystyle\max_{n<l\leq 2n}\big\|\mathcal{P}_{\cdot,-(l-n)}\big(\bm{S}^{t}-\bm{S}^{t,(l)}\big)\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{n\log n}\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}.

(F.6d)

Here, the rotation matrices
\bm{H}^{t,(l)}
and

\bm{R}^{t,(l)}
are defined respectively by

\bm{H}^{t,(l)}\coloneqq\arg\min_{\bm{R}\in\mathcal{O}^{r\times r}}\big\|\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}-\bm{F}^{\star}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\coloneqq\arg\min_{\bm{R}\in\mathcal{O}^{r\times r}}\big\|\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}-\bm{F}^{t}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

F.2
Key lemmas for establishing the induction hypotheses

This subsection establishes the induction hypotheses made in Appendix

F.1
, namely (
F.3
),
(
F.4
) and (
F.6
).
Before continuing, we find it convenient to introduce another function
of
\bm{X}
and
\bm{Y}
(with
\bm{S}
frozen) as follows

f_{\mathsf{aug}}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y};\bm{S}\right)\coloneqq\frac{1}{2p}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}\bm{Y}^{\top}+\bm{S}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2p}\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2p}\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left\|\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}-\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(F.7)

The difference between
f_{\mathsf{aug}}
and
f
lies in the following
balancing term

f_{\mathsf{diff}}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y}\right)\coloneqq-\frac{1}{8}\left\|\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}-\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

that is,
f=f_{\mathsf{aug}}+f_{\mathsf{diff}}
.

The following four lemmas, which are inherited from
[
CCF+20
]

with little modification, are concerned with local strong convexity
as well as the hypotheses (
F.3a
), (
F.3b
),
(
F.3d
), (
F.6b
) and
(
F.3c
).

Lemma 11

(
Restricted strong convexity
)
.

Set

\lambda=C_{\lambda}\sigma\sqrt{np}
for some large enough constant
C_{\lambda}>0
. Suppose that the
sample size obeys
n^{2}p\gg\kappa\mu rn\log n
and that the noise
satisfies
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\ll 1
.
Let the function
f_{\mathsf{aug}}
be defined in (
F.7
).
Then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\left(\bm{\Delta}\right)^{\top}\nabla^{2}f_{\mathsf{aug}}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y};\bm{S}\right)\mathsf{vec}\left(\bm{\Delta}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{10}\sigma_{\min}\left\|\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left\|\nabla^{2}f_{\mathsf{aug}}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y};\bm{S}\right)\right\|,\left\|\nabla^{2}f\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y}\right)\right\|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 10\sigma_{\max}

hold uniformly over all
\bm{X},\bm{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r},\bm{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}

obeying

\left\|\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{X}-\bm{X}^{\star}\\
\bm{Y}-\bm{Y}^{\star}\end{array}\right]\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1000\kappa\sqrt{n}}\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|,\qquad\left\|\bm{S}-\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|\leq C_{\mathrm{S}}\sigma\sqrt{np}

and all
\bm{\Delta}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{X}}\\
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{Y}}\end{array}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{2n\times r}
lying in the set

\left\{\left.\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{X}_{1}\\
\bm{Y}_{1}\end{array}\right]\hat{\bm{H}}-\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{X}_{2}\\
\bm{Y}_{2}\end{array}\right]\,\right|\,\,\left\|\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{X}_{2}-\bm{X}^{\star}\\
\bm{Y}_{2}-\bm{Y}^{\star}\end{array}\right]\right\|\leq\frac{1}{500\kappa}\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|,\hat{\bm{H}}\coloneqq\arg\min_{\bm{R}\in\mathcal{O}^{r\times r}}\left\|\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{X}_{1}\\
\bm{Y}_{1}\end{array}\right]\bm{R}-\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{X}_{2}\\
\bm{Y}_{2}\end{array}\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right\}.

Lemma 12

(
Frobenius norm error w.r.t.
\bm{F}
)
.

Set

\lambda=C_{\lambda}\sigma\sqrt{np}
for some large enough constant
C_{\lambda}>0
. Suppose that the
sample size obeys
n^{2}p\gg\kappa\mu rn\log^{2}n
and that the noise
satisfies
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\ll\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa^{4}\mu r\log n}}
.
If the iterates satisfy (
F.3
) in the
t
th
iteration, then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{F}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}-\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{F}}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

holds as long as
0<\eta\ll 1/(\kappa^{5/2}\sigma_{\max})
.

Lemma 13

(
Spectral norm error w.r.t.
\bm{F}
)
.

Set

\lambda=C_{\lambda}\sigma\sqrt{np}

for some large enough constant
C_{\lambda}>0
. Suppose that the
sample size obeys
n^{2}p\gg\kappa^{4}\mu^{2}r^{2}n\log^{2}n
and
that the noise satisfies
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\ll\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa^{4}\log n}}
.
If the iterates satisfy (
F.3
) in the
t
th
iteration, then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{F}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}-\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{op}}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|

holds as long as
0<\eta\ll 1/(\kappa^{3}\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{r})
and

C_{\mathrm{op}}\gg 1
.

Lemma 14

(
Approximate balancedness
)
.

Set

\lambda=C_{\lambda}\sigma\sqrt{np}

for some large enough constant
C_{\lambda}>0
. Suppose that the
sample size obeys
n^{2}p\gg\kappa^{2}\mu^{2}r^{2}n\log n
and that
the noise satisfies
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\ll\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa^{2}\log n}}
.
If the iterates satisfy (
F.3
) in the
t
th
iteration, then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{X}^{t+1\top}\bm{X}^{t+1}-\bm{Y}^{t+1\top}\bm{Y}^{t+1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{B}}\kappa\eta\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\sqrt{r}\sigma_{\max}^{2}

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq 2n}\big\|\bm{X}^{t+1,(l)\top}\bm{X}^{t+1,(l)}-\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)\top}\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{B}}\kappa\eta\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\sqrt{r}\sigma_{\max}^{2}

hold for some sufficiently large constant
C_{\mathrm{B}}\gg C_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}
,
provided that
0<\eta<1/\sigma_{\min}
.

Lemma 15

(
\ell_{2,\infty}
norm error of leave-one-out
sequences
)
.

Set
\lambda=C_{\lambda}\sigma\sqrt{np}
for some large enough constant

C_{\lambda}>0
. Suppose that the sample size obeys
n^{2}p\gg\kappa^{4}\mu^{2}r^{2}n\log^{3}n

and that the noise satisfies
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\ll\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa^{2}\log n}}
.
If the iterates satisfy (
F.3
) in the
t
th
iteration, then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

\max_{1\leq l\leq 2n}\big\|\big(\bm{F}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{H}^{t+1,(l)}-\bm{F}^{\star}\big)_{l,\cdot}\big\|_{2}\leq C_{2}\kappa\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}

holds, provided that
0<\eta\ll 1/(\kappa^{2}\sqrt{r}\sigma_{\max})
,

C_{\mathrm{op}}\gg 1
and
C_{2}\gg C_{\mathrm{op}}
.

Lemma 16

(
\ell_{2,\infty}
norm error of the true
sequence
)
.

Set
\lambda=C_{\lambda}\sigma\sqrt{np}
for some large enough constant

C_{\lambda}>0
. Suppose that
n\geq\mu r
and that the noise satisfies

\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\ll\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa^{2}\log n}}
.
If the iterates satisfy (
F.3
) and (
F.6
)
in the
t
th iteration, then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-99})
,
one has

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{F}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}-\bm{F}^{\star}\big\|_{\mathrm{2,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\infty}\kappa\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty},

provided that
C_{\infty}\geq 5C_{1}+C_{2}
.

Proof of Lemmas
11
,
12
,

13
,
14
,
15

and
16
.

As it turns out, Lemmas
11
,

12
,
13
and
14

follow immediately from the proofs of
[
CCF+20
, Lemmas 17, 10, 11, 15]

respectively. More specifically, the proofs can be accomplished by
replacing
\bm{E}
in the proofs therein with
\tilde{\bm{E}}\coloneqq\bm{E}+\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{S}^{t}
.
To see this, we remark that the only property of the perturbation
matrix
\bm{E}
utilized in the proofs therein is that
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{E})\|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{np}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
; under our hypotheses, the
new matrix
\tilde{\bm{E}}
clearly satisfies this property since

\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\bigl(\tilde{\bm{E}}\bigr)\|\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|+\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{S}^{t}\right\|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{np}.

Regarding Lemma
15
, we note that
\bm{S}_{l,\cdot}^{t,(l)}\equiv\bm{S}_{l,\cdot}^{\star}

by construction. Therefore, the update rule regarding the
l
th row
of
\{\bm{X}_{l,\cdot}^{t,(l)}\}_{t\geq 0}
and
\{\bm{Y}_{l,\cdot}^{t,(l)}\}_{t\geq 0}

is exactly the same as that in the leave-one-out sequence introduced
in
[
CCF+20
]
. Thus, Lemma
15

follows immediately from the proof of
[
CCF+20
, Lemma 13]
.

Finally, the proof of Lemma
16
is exactly
the same as the proof of
[
CCF+20
, Lemma 14]
.∎

Next, we justify the hypotheses (
F.3e
), (
F.6a
)
and (
F.6c
) in the following three lemmas, which
require more careful analysis of the properties about
\{\bm{S}^{t}\}
.

Lemma 17

(
Spectral norm error w.r.t.
\bm{S}
)
.

Set
\tau=C_{\tau}\sigma\sqrt{\log n}

for some large enough constant
C_{\tau}>0
. Suppose that the
sample size obeys
n^{2}p\gg\kappa^{4}\mu rn\log n
, the noise satisfies
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\ll 1/\sqrt{\kappa^{2}\log n}
, the outlier
fraction satisfies
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\ll 1/\sqrt{\kappa^{5}\mu r\log^{2}n}

and
n^{2}p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\gg\mu nr\log^{2}n
. If the iterates satisfy (
F.3b
) and (
F.3c
)
in the
(t+1)
-th iteration, then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

\left\|\bm{S}^{t+1}-\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|\leq C_{\mathrm{S}}\sigma\sqrt{np}

holds for some constant
C_{\mathrm{S}}>0
that does not rely on
the choice of other constants.

Proof.

See Appendix

F.4
.∎

Lemma 18

(
Leave-one-out perturbation w.r.t.
\bm{F}
)
.

Set

\lambda=C_{\lambda}\sigma\sqrt{np}

for some large enough constant
C_{\lambda}>0
. Suppose that the
sample size obeys
n^{2}p\gg\kappa^{4}\mu^{2}r^{2}n\log^{4}n
, the noise satisfies
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\ll 1/\sqrt{\kappa^{4}\mu r\log n}
, the outlier fraction satisfies
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\ll 1/(\kappa^{3}\mu r\log n)

and
n^{2}p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\gg\mu rn\log n
. If the iterates satisfy
(
F.3
) and (
F.6
)
in the
t
th iteration, then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

\max_{1\leq l\leq 2n}\big\|\bm{F}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}-\bm{F}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{R}^{t+1,(l)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C_{1}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}

holds for some constant
C_{1}>0
, provided that
\eta\ll 1/(n\kappa^{2}\sigma_{\max})

and
C_{1}\gg C_{3}
.

Proof.

See Appendix
F.5
.∎

Lemma 19

(
Leave-one-out perturbation w.r.t.
\bm{S}
)
.

Set
\tau=C_{\tau}\sigma\sqrt{\log n}

for some large enough constant
C_{\tau}>0
. Suppose that the
sample size satisfies
n^{2}p\gg\kappa^{4}\mu^{2}r^{2}n\log n
, the noise obeys
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\ll 1/\sqrt{\kappa^{2}\log n}
and the outlier
fraction satisfies
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\ll 1/\kappa
.
If the iterates satisfy (
F.3b
), (
F.3c
)
and (
F.6a
) in the
(t+1)
-th iteration,
then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq n}\big\|\mathcal{P}_{-l,\cdot}\big(\bm{S}^{t+1}-\bm{S}^{t+1,(l)}\big)\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{n\log n}\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty},

\displaystyle\max_{n<l\leq 2n}\big\|\mathcal{P}_{\cdot,-(l-n)}\big(\bm{S}^{t+1}-\bm{S}^{t+1,(l)}\big)\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{n\log n}\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}

hold for some constant
C_{3}
that does not rely on the choice of other constants.

Proof.

See Appendix

F.6
.∎

Finally, it remains to justify (
F.4
), which
is a straightforward consequence from standard gradient descent theory
and implies the existence of a point with nearly zero gradient.

Lemma 20

(
Monotonicity of the function values
)
.

Set

\lambda=C_{\lambda}\sigma\sqrt{np}

for some large enough constant
C_{\lambda}>0
. Suppose that the
noise satisfies
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\ll 1
.
If the iterates satisfy (
F.3
) in the
t
th
iteration, then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

F\left(\bm{X}^{t+1},\bm{Y}^{t+1};\bm{S}^{t+1}\right)\leq F\left(\bm{X}^{t},\bm{Y}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)-\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|\nabla f\left(\bm{X}^{t},\bm{Y}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

holds as long as
\eta\ll 1/(\kappa n\sigma_{\max})
.

Proof.

See
Appendix
F.7
. ∎

F.3
Proof of Lemma
10

Summing (
F.4
) over
t=1,\ldots,t_{0}
gives

\displaystyle F\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}},\bm{Y}^{t_{0}},\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq F\left(\bm{X}^{0},\bm{Y}^{0},\bm{S}^{0}\right)-\frac{\eta}{2}\sum_{t=0}^{t_{0}-1}\left\|\nabla f\left(\bm{X}^{t},\bm{Y}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

which further implies

\displaystyle\min_{0\leq t<t_{0}}\left\|\nabla f\left(\bm{X}^{t},\bm{Y}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{t_{0}}\sum_{t=0}^{t_{0}-1}\left\|\nabla f\left(\bm{X}^{t},\bm{Y}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\eta t_{0}}\left[F\left(\bm{X}^{\star},\bm{Y}^{\star},\bm{S}^{\star}\right)-F\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}},\bm{Y}^{t_{0}},\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\right)\right].

(F.8)

Here, the last inequality results from our choice
(\bm{X}^{0},\bm{Y}^{0},\bm{S}^{0})=(\bm{X}^{\star},\bm{Y}^{\star},\bm{S}^{\star})
.
Therefore, it suffices to control
F\left(\bm{X}^{\star},\bm{Y}^{\star},\bm{S}^{\star}\right)-F\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}},\bm{Y}^{t_{0}},\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\right)
.

We first decompose the difference into

\displaystyle F\left(\bm{X}^{\star},\bm{Y}^{\star},\bm{S}^{\star}\right)-F\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}},\bm{Y}^{t_{0}},\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\right)=f\left(\bm{X}^{\star},\bm{Y}^{\star};\bm{S}^{\star}\right)+\frac{\tau}{p}\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|_{1}-f\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}},\bm{Y}^{t_{0}};\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\right)-\frac{\tau}{p}\left\|\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad=\underbrace{\vphantom{\frac{\tau}{p}}f\left(\bm{X}^{\star},\bm{Y}^{\star};\bm{S}^{\star}\right)-f\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}},\bm{Y}^{t_{0}};\bm{S}^{\star}\right)}_{\eqqcolon\Delta_{1}}+\underbrace{\vphantom{\frac{\tau}{p}}f\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}},\bm{Y}^{t_{0}};\bm{S}^{\star}\right)-f\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}},\bm{Y}^{t_{0}};\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\right)}_{\eqqcolon\Delta_{2}}+\underbrace{\frac{\tau}{p}\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|_{1}-\frac{\tau}{p}\left\|\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\right\|_{1}}_{\eqqcolon\Delta_{3}}.

In what follows, we shall bound
\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2}
and
\Delta_{3}

separately.

1.

In view of the proof of
[
CCF+20
, Lemma 9]
, we have

\left|\Delta_{1}\right|\lesssim r\kappa^{2}\left(\frac{\lambda}{p}\right)^{2},

provided that
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\ll\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa^{4}\mu r\log n}}
.

2.

When it comes to
\Delta_{2}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\Delta_{2}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{Y}^{t_{0}\top}+\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2p}\|\bm{F}^{t_{0}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{Y}^{t_{0}\top}+\bm{S}^{t_{0}}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{\lambda}{2p}\|\bm{F}^{t_{0}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{Y}^{t_{0}\top}+\bm{S}^{t_{0}}-\bm{M}\right),\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{Y}^{t_{0}\top}+\bm{S}^{t_{0}}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

(F.9)

where the last step arises from the elementary inequality
\langle\bm{A},\bm{B}\rangle\leq\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

and the triangle inequality. It is straightforward to derive from
(
F.3e
) that

\|\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{n}\|\bm{S}^{\star}-\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\|\lesssim\sigma n\sqrt{p}.

Moving on to the first term in (
F.9
), one has by the
triangle inequality

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{Y}^{t_{0}\top}+\bm{S}^{t_{0}}-\bm{M}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{Y}^{t_{0}\top}-\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{S}^{t_{0}}-\bm{S}^{\star}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{Y}^{t_{0}\top}-\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\sigma n\sqrt{p},

where the last bound follows from Lemma
1

and the bound above
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{S}^{t_{0}}-\bm{S}^{\star})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|\bm{S}^{t_{0}}-\bm{S}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\sigma n\sqrt{p}
.
We can further decompose
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{Y}^{t_{0}\top}-\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top})\|_{\mathrm{F}}

into

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{Y}^{t_{0}\top}-\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{H}^{t_{0}}-\bm{X}^{\star}\right)\bm{Y}^{\star\top}\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left[\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{H}^{t_{0}}\left(\bm{Y}^{t_{0}}\bm{H}^{t_{0}}-\bm{Y}^{\star}\right)^{\top}\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\text{i})}{\lesssim}\sqrt{\kappa p}\left\|\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{H}^{t_{0}}-\bm{X}^{\star}\right)\bm{Y}^{\star\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\sqrt{\kappa p}\left\|\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{H}^{t_{0}}\left(\bm{Y}^{t_{0}}\bm{H}^{t_{0}}-\bm{Y}^{\star}\right)^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa p}\left\|\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{H}^{t_{0}}-\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left\|\bm{Y}^{\star}\right\|+\sqrt{\kappa p}\left\|\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{H}^{t_{0}}\right\|\left\|\bm{Y}^{t_{0}}\bm{H}^{t_{0}}-\bm{Y}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\text{ii})}{\lesssim}\sqrt{\kappa p}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|\lesssim\kappa^{3/2}\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{p}}\sqrt{r}.

(F.10)

Here, the relation (i) utilizes Lemma
4
,
and the facts that
(\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{H}^{t_{0}}-\bm{X}^{\star})\bm{Y}^{\star\top}\in T^{\star}

and that
\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{H}^{t_{0}}(\bm{Y}^{t_{0}}\bm{H}^{t_{0}}-\bm{Y}^{\star})^{\top}\in T^{t_{0}}
,
where
T^{t_{0}}
denotes the tangent space at
\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{Y}^{t_{0}\top}
.
In addition, the last line (ii) holds because of the hypothesis (
F.3a
)
and the simple fact
\|\bm{X}^{t_{0}}\bm{H}^{t_{0}}\|\leq 2\|\bm{X}^{\star}\|
, which is an immediate consequence of the hypothesis (
F.3b
) provided that
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\ll 1
.
Collecting the bounds together, we arrive at

\left|\Delta_{2}\right|\lesssim\left(\kappa^{3/2}\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{p}}\sqrt{r}+\sigma n\sqrt{p}\right)\cdot\sigma n\sqrt{p}+\sigma^{2}n^{2}p+\frac{\lambda}{p}\cdot\kappa\frac{\lambda}{p}r\lesssim\sigma^{2}n^{2}p,

with the proviso that
np\gg\kappa^{3}r
.

3.

In the end, we have the following upper bound on
\Delta_{3}
:

\left|\Delta_{3}\right|\leq\frac{\tau}{p}\left\|\bm{S}^{t_{0}}-\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|_{1}\leq\frac{\tau}{p}n\left\|\bm{S}^{t_{0}}-\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\frac{1}{p}\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{np/\log n}}\sigma n^{2}\sqrt{p}\asymp\frac{\lambda}{p}\sigma n^{3/2}\sqrt{\log n},

where we have made use of the elementary fact that
\|\bm{A}\|_{1}\leq n\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

for all
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
.

Putting the above bounds together, one can reach

\left|F\left(\bm{X}^{\star},\bm{Y}^{\star},\bm{S}^{\star}\right)-F\left(\bm{X}^{t_{0}},\bm{Y}^{t_{0}},\bm{S}^{t_{0}}\right)\right|\lesssim r\kappa^{2}\left(\frac{\lambda}{p}\right)^{2}+\sigma^{2}n^{2}p+\frac{\lambda}{p}\sigma n^{3/2}\sqrt{\log n}\lesssim n\left(\frac{\lambda}{p}\right)^{2}\sqrt{\log n}

as long as
n\gg\kappa^{2}r
and
\lambda\asymp\sigma\sqrt{np}
.
Substitution into (
F.8
) allows us to conclude
that

\min_{0\leq t\leq t_{0}}\left\|\nabla f\left(\bm{X}^{t},\bm{Y}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{1}{\eta t_{0}}n\left(\frac{\lambda}{p}\right)^{2}\sqrt{\log n}}\leq\frac{1}{n^{20}}\frac{\lambda}{p}\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}},

provided that
\eta\asymp 1/(n\kappa^{3}\sigma_{\max})
,
t_{0}\geq n^{47}

and
n\geq\kappa
.

F.4
Proof of Lemma
17

In view of the definitions
\Omega^{\star}=\{(i,j):S_{ij}^{\star}\neq 0\}\subseteq\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}\subseteq\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}

and
\bm{S}^{t+1}=\mathcal{S}_{\tau}[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}(\bm{L}^{\star}+\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top})]
,
we have the decomposition

\displaystyle\bm{S}^{t+1}-\bm{S}^{\star}=\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{S}^{t+1}\right)-\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{S}^{t+1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\underbrace{\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}+\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top}\right)\right]-\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}\right)}_{\eqqcolon\bm{A}^{t+1}}+\underbrace{\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}+\bm{E}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top}\right)\right]}_{\eqqcolon\bm{B}^{t+1}}.

(F.11)

We shall control
\|\bm{A}^{t+1}\|
and
\|\bm{B}^{t+1}\|
separately.

1.

We begin by controlling the size of
\bm{A}^{t+1}
, which can be
further decomposed into

\displaystyle\bm{A}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{E}\right)+\underbrace{\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}\right)\right]-\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}\right)}_{\eqqcolon\bm{A}_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top}+\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}\right)\right]-\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}\right)\right]}_{\eqqcolon\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}}.

(F.12)

First of all, we know that
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}(\bm{E})\|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{np\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\leq\sigma\sqrt{np}
,
as long as
n^{2}p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\gg n\log^{2}n
. This arises
from standard concentration results for the spectral norm of sub-Gaussian
random matrices (cf. Lemma
1
). Regarding
\bm{A}_{1}
,
we know from the definition of
\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(\cdot)
that
\|\bm{A}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq\tau
.
More precisely, we have

\left(\bm{A}_{1}\right)_{ij}=\begin{cases}-\tau&\text{if}\ S_{ij}^{\star}+E_{ij}\geq\tau,\\
-S_{ij}^{\star}-E_{ij}&\text{if}\ -\tau<S_{ij}^{\star}+E_{ij}<\tau,\\
\tau&\text{if}\ S_{ij}^{\star}+E_{ij}\leq-\tau.\end{cases}

Recall from Assumption
4
that
\bm{S}^{\star}

has random signs on its support
\Omega^{\star}\subseteq\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}

and
E_{ij}
is symmetric around zero. It then follows from standard
concentration results for the spectral norm of matrices with i.i.d. entries
that

\left\|\bm{A}_{1}\right\|\lesssim\tau\sqrt{np\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}=C_{\tau}\sigma\sqrt{np\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\log n},

provided that
n^{2}p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\gg n\log^{2}n
. Moving on to
\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}
, since it is supported
on
\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}
, we can further decompose
\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\|

into

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|=\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right)-p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|.

(F.13)

Invoking Lemma
5
with
\bm{A}=\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}
,

\bm{B}=\bm{I}_{n}
and
\rho_{0}=p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}
, we have

\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right)-p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|\leq C\sqrt{np\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|_{2,\infty},

(F.14)

with the proviso that
n^{2}p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\gg n\log n
. Combine
the above bounds to reach

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|+C\sqrt{np\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|+C\sqrt{np\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|_{2,\infty},

as soon as
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\leq 1/2
. We
are then in need of an upper bound on
\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\|_{2,\infty}
,
which is supplied in the following fact.

Fact 3
.

Suppose that
n^{2}p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\gg\mu rn\log n
and
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\ll 1/\kappa
. Then with probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-100})
,
one has

\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\sqrt{40\kappa p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(C_{\infty}\kappa+2C_{\mathrm{op}}\right)\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|.

With the help of Fact
3
, we can continue
the upper bound as follows

\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|\leq 2C\sqrt{np\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\left(C_{\infty}+C_{\mathrm{op}}\right)\sqrt{np}\kappa^{3/2}\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{n\log n}\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|.

All in all, we obtain the following bound on
\bm{A}^{t+1}
:

\displaystyle\|\bm{A}^{t+1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}(\bm{E})\|+\|\bm{A}_{1}\|+\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{np}+C_{\tau}\sigma\sqrt{np\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\log n}+\left(C_{\infty}+C_{\mathrm{op}}\right)\sqrt{np}\kappa^{3/2}\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{n\log n}\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{S}\sigma\sqrt{np},

with the proviso that
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\ll 1/\sqrt{\kappa^{5}\mu r\log^{2}n}.

Here, the last line uses the incoherence assumption
\|\bm{F}^{\star}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\sqrt{\mu r/n}\|\bm{X}^{\star}\|
(cf. (
B.1
)).

2.

When it comes to
\bm{B}^{t+1}
, we first note that

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top}\right\|_{\infty}=\left\|\left(\bm{X}^{\star}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\right)\bm{Y}^{\star\top}+\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\left(\bm{Y}^{\star}-\bm{Y}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\right)^{\top}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\bm{Y}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}+\left\|\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\bm{Y}^{\star}-\bm{Y}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\right\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 3C_{\infty}\kappa\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\leq 3C_{\infty}\kappa\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\frac{\mu r}{n}\sigma_{\max}.

(F.15)

Here, we have plugged in (
F.3c
) for the

(t+1)
-th iteration and its immediate consequence
\|\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\|\bm{F}^{t+1}\|_{2,\infty}\leq 2\|\bm{F}^{\star}\|_{2,\infty}
,
as long as
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\ll 1/\kappa
.
As a result, for all
(i,j)
we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(\bm{M}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top}\right)_{ij}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left(\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}+\bm{E}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top}\right)_{ij}\right|\leq\big|E_{ij}\big|+\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\leq}\big|E_{ij}\big|+3C_{\infty}\kappa\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\frac{\mu r}{n}\sigma_{\max}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(ii)}}{\leq}C_{\lambda}\sigma\sqrt{\log n}=\tau.

Here, the inequality (i) comes from (
F.15
),
and the last line (ii) relies on the property of sub-Gaussian random
variables (namely,
|E_{ij}|\leq\tau/2
with probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-102})
) and
the sample size condition
n^{2}p\gg\kappa^{4}\mu^{2}r^{2}n\log n
.
An immediate consequence is that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-100})
,

\bm{B}^{t+1}=\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}\backslash\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}+\bm{E}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top}\right)\right]=\bm{0}.

(F.16)

Substituting the above two bounds into (
F.11
),
we conclude that
\left\|\bm{S}^{t+1}-\bm{S}^{\star}\right\|\leq C_{S}\sigma\sqrt{np}

as claimed.

Proof of Fact
3
.

In view of the
definition of
\bm{A}^{t+1}
in (
F.12
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t+1}\right\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top}-\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}\right)\right\|_{2,\infty},

where we use the non-expansiveness of the proximal operator
\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(\cdot)
.
Apply a similar argument as in bounding (
F.10
)
to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top}-\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}\right)\right\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}-\bm{X}^{\star}\right)\bm{Y}^{\star\top}\right]\right\|_{2,\infty}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left[\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\left(\bm{Y}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}-\bm{Y}^{\star}\right)^{\top}\right]\right\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sqrt{40\kappa p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left\|\left(\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}-\bm{X}^{\star}\right)\bm{Y}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}+\sqrt{40\kappa p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left\|\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\left(\bm{Y}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}-\bm{Y}^{\star}\right)^{\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sqrt{40\kappa p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(C_{\infty}\kappa+2C_{\mathrm{op}}\right)\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|,

as long as
n^{2}p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\gg\mu rn\log n
. Here, the
last line uses the induction hypotheses (
F.3b
)
and (
F.3c
) for the
(t+1)
-th iteration
and their immediate consequence
\|\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\|_{2,\infty}\leq 2\|\bm{F}^{\star}\|_{2,\infty}
, as long as
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\ll 1/\kappa
. Taking the preceding two bounds together concludes the proof. ∎

F.5
Proof of Lemma
18

Without loss of generality, we only consider the case when
1\leq l\leq n
.
The case with
n+1\leq l\leq 2n
can be derived similarly with very
minor modification, and hence we omit it for the sake of brevity.

To begin with, since
(\bm{H}^{t+1},\bm{R}^{t+1,(l)})
is the choice
of the rotation matrix that best aligns
\bm{F}^{t+1}
and
\bm{F}^{t+1,(l)}
,
we have

\big\|\bm{F}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}-\bm{F}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{R}^{t+1,(l)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\big\|\bm{F}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t}-\bm{F}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

In view of the gradient update rule, one has

\displaystyle\bm{F}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t}-\bm{F}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}

\displaystyle\quad=\left[\bm{F}^{t}-\eta\nabla f\left(\bm{F}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)\right]\bm{H}^{t}-\left[\bm{F}^{t,(l)}-\eta\nabla f^{(l)}\big(\bm{F}^{t,(l)};\bm{S}^{t,(l)}\big)\right]\bm{R}^{t,(l)}

\displaystyle\quad=\bm{F}^{t}\bm{H}^{t}-\eta\nabla f\left(\bm{F}^{t}\bm{H}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)-\left[\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}-\eta\nabla f^{(l)}(\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)};\bm{S}^{t,(l)})\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\underbrace{\bm{F}^{t}\bm{H}^{t}-\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}-\eta\left[\nabla f_{\mathsf{aug}}\big(\bm{F}^{t}\bm{H}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\big)-\nabla f_{\mathsf{aug}}\big(\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)};\bm{S}^{t}\big)\right]}_{\eqqcolon\bm{C}_{1}}-\underbrace{\eta\left[\nabla f_{\mathsf{diff}}\left(\bm{F}^{t}\bm{H}^{t}\right)-\nabla f_{\mathsf{diff}}\big(\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\big)\right]}_{\eqqcolon\bm{C}_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\underbrace{\eta\left[\nabla f^{(l)}\big(\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)};\bm{S}^{t,(l)}\big)-\nabla f\big(\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)};\bm{S}^{t,(l)}\big)\right]}_{\eqqcolon\bm{C}_{3}}+\underbrace{\eta\left[\nabla f\big(\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)};\bm{S}^{t,(l)}\big)-\nabla f\big(\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)};\bm{S}^{t}\big)\right]}_{\eqqcolon\bm{C}_{4}}.

Here, the second identity relies on the facts that
\nabla f(\bm{F};\bm{S})\bm{R}=\nabla f(\bm{F}\bm{R};\bm{S})

and
\nabla f^{(l)}(\bm{F};\bm{S})\bm{R}=\nabla f^{(l)}(\bm{F}\bm{R};\bm{S})

for any orthonormal matrix
\bm{R}\in\mathcal{O}^{r\times r}
. We
shall then control
\bm{C}_{1}
,
\bm{C}_{2}
,
\bm{C}_{3}
and

\bm{C}_{4}
separately.

Employing the same strategy used to bound
\bm{A}_{1}
and
\bm{A}_{2}

in the proof of
[
CCF+20
, Lemma 12]
, we can demonstrate
that

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{C}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{20}\eta\right)\big\|\bm{F}^{t}\bm{H}^{t}-\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\bm{C}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\eta\left(\sigma\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty},

provided that
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\ll 1/\sqrt{\kappa^{4}\mu r\log n}

and
\eta\ll 1/(n\kappa^{2}\sigma_{\max})
. With regards to
\bm{C}_{3}
,
it is seen from the definitions of
\nabla f
and
\nabla f^{(l)}

that

\bm{C}_{3}=\eta\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\left[\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\left(\bm{X}^{t,(l)}\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}-\bm{L}^{\star}\right)-p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}})_{l,\cdot}}\left(\bm{X}^{t,(l)}\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}-\bm{L}^{\star}\right)\right]\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}+p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}})_{l,\cdot}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\\
\left[\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\left(\bm{X}^{t,(l)}\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}-\bm{L}^{\star}\right)-p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}})_{l,\cdot}}\left(\bm{X}^{t,(l)}\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}-\bm{L}^{\star}\right)\right]^{\top}\bm{X}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}+p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{(\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}})_{l,\cdot}}\left(\bm{E}\right)^{\top}\bm{X}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\end{array}\right],

which has the same form as
\bm{A}_{3}
in the proof of
[
CCF+20
, Lemma 12]
.
It thus follows from
[
CCF+20
, Claim 5, 6 and 7]
that

\left\|\bm{C}_{3}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\eta\sigma\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}+\eta\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}\log n}{np}}\left\|\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}-\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\sigma_{\max},

provided that
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\ll\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa^{2}\log n}}

and that
n^{2}p\gg n\log^{3}n
.

We are then left with controlling the term
\bm{C}_{4}
. Towards
this, we invoke the definition of
f
to decompose

\displaystyle\bm{C}_{4}

\displaystyle=\eta\left[\begin{array}[]{c}p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\right)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\\
p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}}\left(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\right)^{\top}\bm{X}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{\eta}{p}\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{P}_{-l,\cdot}\big(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\big)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\\
\big[\mathcal{P}_{-l,\cdot}\big(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\big)\big]^{\top}\bm{X}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\end{array}\right]}_{\eqqcolon\bm{D}_{1}}+\underbrace{\frac{\eta}{p}\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\left(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\right)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\\
\big[\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\big(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\big)\big]^{\top}\bm{X}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\end{array}\right]}_{\eqqcolon\bm{D}_{2}}.

Here, we have used the fact that both
\bm{S}^{t,(l)}
and
\bm{S}^{t}

are supported on
\Omega^{\star}\subseteq\Omega_{\mathsf{obs}}
.
Regarding the first matrix
\bm{D}_{1}
, we have the following fact.

Fact 4
.

Suppose that the sample size obeys
n^{2}p\gg\kappa^{4}\mu^{2}r^{2}n\log n
, the noise satisfies
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\ll 1/\kappa
, the outlier fraction satisfies
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\ll 1/\kappa^{3}

and
n^{2}p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\gg\mu rn\log n
hold. Then with probability
at least
1-O(n^{-}100)
, we have

\bigl\|\bm{D}_{1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\eta\sigma\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\bigl\|\bm{F}^{\star}\bigr\|_{2,\infty}.

With regards to
\bm{D}_{2}
, recall that
\bm{S}_{l,\cdot}^{t,(l)}=\bm{S}_{l,\cdot}^{\star}
.
Using the decomposition (
F.11
) in the
proof of Lemma
17
, and recalling that
\bm{B}^{t+1}=\bm{0}

from the proof of Lemma
17
, we obtain

\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\big(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\big)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}=\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\big(\bm{A}_{1}+\bm{E}\big)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}+\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\big(\bm{A}_{2}^{t}\big)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)},

(F.17)

where

\displaystyle\bm{A}_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}\right)\right]-\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}\right);

\displaystyle\bm{A}_{2}^{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}-\bm{X}^{t}\bm{Y}^{t\top}+\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}\right)\right]-\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}\right)\right].

For the first term
\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}(\bm{A}_{1}+\bm{E})\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}
,
the independence between
\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}
and the
l
-th
row of
\bm{A}_{1}+\bm{E}
allows us to obtain the following bound.

Fact 5
.

Suppose that
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\ll 1/\log n

and that
n^{2}p\gg n\log^{4}n
. Then with probability
at least
1-O(n^{-}100)
, we have

\left\|\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\left(\bm{A}_{1}\right)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{np\log n}\left\|\bm{Y}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}.

The term involving
\bm{A}_{2}^{t}
is controlled in
the following claim, which relies heavily on the small scale of the
entries in
\bm{A}_{2}^{t}
.

Fact 6
.

Suppose that
n\gg\kappa\mu r
,
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\ll 1/\kappa
,
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\ll 1/(\kappa\mu r)

and that
n^{2}p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\gg n\log n
. Then with probability
at least
1-O(n^{-}100)
, we have

\left\|\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\left(\bm{A}_{2}^{t}\right)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{np\log n}\left\|\bm{Y}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}.

Combining the two bounds in Facts
5
and
6

gives

\left\|\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\big(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\big)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{np\log n}\left\|\bm{Y}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}.

The same bound applies to
\|\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t})^{\top}\bm{X}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

via the same technique. As a result, we have

\|\bm{D}_{2}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\eta\sigma\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\|\bm{F}^{\star}\|_{2,\infty}.

Putting the above bounds together yields

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{F}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}-\bm{F}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{R}^{t+1,(l)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|\bm{C}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\bm{C}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\bm{C}_{3}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\bm{D}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\bm{D}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(1-\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{20}\eta\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{t}\bm{H}^{t}-\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\eta\left(\sigma\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\tilde{C}\left(\eta\sigma\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}+\eta\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}\log n}{np}}\left\|\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}-\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\sigma_{\max}\right)+\tilde{C}\eta\sigma\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\bigl\|\bm{F}^{\star}\bigr\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\text{(i)}}{\leq}\left(1-\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{20}\eta\right)C_{1}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}+\eta\left(\sigma\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}+\tilde{C}\frac{\lambda}{p}\bigl\|\bm{F}^{\star}\bigr\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\tilde{C}\eta\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}\log n}{np}}\left(C_{\infty}\kappa+C_{1}\right)\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\sigma_{\max}+\tilde{C}\eta\sigma\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\bigl\|\bm{F}^{\star}\bigr\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\text{(ii)}}{\leq}C_{1}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty},

where (i) invokes (
F.6a
) and its immediate
consequence that

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}-\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{F}^{t}\bm{H}^{t}-\bm{F}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\bm{F}^{t}\bm{H}^{t}-\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}

(F.18)

\displaystyle\leq\left(C_{\infty}\kappa+C_{1}\right)\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}.

(F.19)

The last line (ii) holds as long as
n^{2}p\gg\kappa^{4}\mu^{2}r^{2}n\log n

and
C_{1}
is large enough.

Proof of Fact
4
.

First notice that
\bm{S}^{t}

is supported on
\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}
, which is a consequence of
(
F.11
) and (
F.16
) as long as
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\ll 1/\kappa
and
n^{2}p\gg\kappa^{4}\mu^{2}r^{2}n\log n
.
By replacing
\bm{X}^{t+1}
(resp.
\bm{Y}^{t+1})
with
\bm{X}^{t+1,(l)}
(resp.
\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)})
) and invoking (
F.19
) instead of (
F.3c
), the same arguments yield the fact that
\bm{S}^{t,(l)}
is also supported
on
\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}
. Define
\omega_{ij}\coloneqq\ind_{(i,j)\in\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}
.
The Frobenius norm of the upper block of
\bm{D}_{1}
can be bounded
by

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{-l,\cdot}\big(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\big)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\sum_{i:i\neq l}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\right)_{ik}Y_{kj}^{t,(l)}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i:i\neq l}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\omega_{ik}\left(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\right)_{ik}Y_{kj}^{t,(l)}\right]^{2}\leq\sum_{i:i\neq l}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\omega_{ik}\left(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\right)_{ik}^{2}\right]\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\omega_{ik}\bigl(Y_{kj}^{t,(l)}\bigr)^{2}\right],

where we use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in the last step. Converting
to the matrix notation, we obtain

\sum_{k=1}^{n}\omega_{ik}\bigl(Y_{kj}^{t,(l)}\bigr)^{2}=\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{e}_{i}\bm{e}_{j}^{\top}\bm{Y}^{t,(l)\top}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Applying a similar argument as in bounding (
F.10
),
one can obtain from Lemma
4

that

\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{e}_{i}\bm{e}_{j}^{\top}\bm{Y}^{t,(l)\top}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\kappa p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\bigl\|\bm{Y}_{\cdot,j}^{t,(l)}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\kappa p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\|\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

provided that
n^{2}p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\gg\mu rn\log n
. This allows
us to reach

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{-l,\cdot}\big(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\big)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{i:i\neq l}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\omega_{ik}\left(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\right)_{ik}^{2}\right]}\cdot\sqrt{\kappa p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\bigl\|\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\kappa p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\bigl\|\mathcal{P}_{-l,\cdot}\left(\bm{S}^{t,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t}\right)\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}\bigl\|\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\sqrt{\kappa p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{n\log n}\bigl\|\bm{F}^{\star}\bigr\|^{2}\bigl\|\bm{F}^{\star}\bigr\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{np\log n}\bigl\|\bm{F}^{\star}\bigr\|_{2,\infty}.

Here, the penultimate step comes from the hypothesis (
F.6c
),
whereas the last step holds as long as
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\ll 1/\kappa^{3}
.
The Frobenius norm of the lower block of
\bm{D}_{1}
admits the
same bound. As a result, we obtain
\bigl\|\bm{D}_{1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\eta\sigma\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\bigl\|\bm{F}^{\star}\bigr\|_{2,\infty}

as claimed. ∎

Proof of Fact
5
.

Regarding the first
term on the right-hand side of (
F.17
), we can
write

\big\|\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\big(\bm{A}_{1}+\bm{E}\big)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{j=1}^{n}\big(\bm{A}_{1}+\bm{E}\big)_{lj}\bm{Y}_{j,\cdot}^{t,(l)}\Big\|_{2}=\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{j=1}^{n}\underbrace{\omega_{lj}\left[\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(S_{lj}^{\star}+E_{ij})-S_{lj}^{\star}\right]\bm{Y}_{j,\cdot}^{t,(l)}}_{\eqqcolon\bm{u}_{j}}\Big\|_{2},

where
\omega_{lj}\coloneqq\ind\{(l,j)\in\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}\}
is a Bernoulli
random variable with mean
p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}
. Since
\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}

is independent of
\{\omega_{lj}\}_{1\leq j\leq n}
and
\bm{S}_{l,\cdot}^{\star}
,
the vectors
\{\bm{u}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}
are statistically independent
conditional on
\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}
. We can thus apply the matrix Bernstein
inequality to control this term. Specifically, conditional on
\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}
,
we have

\left\|\|\bm{u}_{j}\|_{2}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\big\|\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\big\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\omega_{lj}\left[\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(S_{lj}^{\star}+E_{ij})-S_{lj}^{\star}\right]\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\overset{\text{(i)}}{\lesssim}\tau\left\|\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\right\|_{2,\infty},

V\coloneqq\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\sum\nolimits_{j=1}^{n}\omega_{lj}^{2}\left(\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(S_{lj}^{\star}+E_{ij})-S_{lj}^{\star}\right)^{2}\bm{Y}_{j,\cdot}^{t,(l)}\bm{Y}_{j,\cdot}^{t,(l)\top}\right]\right\|\overset{(\text{ii})}{\lesssim}p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\tau^{2}\big\|\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

where
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{1}}
denotes the sub-exponential norm
[
Ver17
]
.
Here, the relation (i) holds since

\displaystyle\left\|\omega_{lj}\left[\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(S_{lj}^{\star}+E_{ij})-S_{lj}^{\star}\right]\right\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(S_{lj}^{\star}+E_{ij})-S_{lj}^{\star}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\left\|\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(S_{lj}^{\star}+E_{ij})-\left(S_{lj}^{\star}+E_{ij}\right)\right\|_{\psi_{1}}+\left\|E_{ij}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(S_{lj}^{\star}+E_{ij})-\left(S_{lj}^{\star}+E_{ij}\right)\right|+\left\|E_{ij}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq 2\tau,

where we have used the fact that
|\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(x)-x|\leq\tau

and
\|E_{ij}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\|E_{ij}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\sigma\leq\tau
.
In addition, the second inequality (ii) comes from the identity
\mathbb{E}[\omega_{lj}^{2}]=p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}

and the fact that

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(S_{lj}^{\star}+E_{ij})-S_{lj}^{\star}\right)^{2}\right]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(S_{lj}^{\star}+E_{ij})-S_{lj}^{\star}-E_{ij}\right)^{2}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[E_{ij}^{2}\right]\lesssim\tau^{2}.

With the aid of the above bounds, we can invoke the matrix Bernstein
inequality
[
KLT11
, Proposition 2]
to reach

\displaystyle\left\|\sum\nolimits_{j=1}^{n}\bm{u}_{j}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{V\log n}+\left\|\|\bm{u}_{j}\|_{2}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\log^{2}n

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\tau^{2}\left\|\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\log n}+\tau\big\|\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\big\|_{2,\infty}\log^{2}n

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\tau\sqrt{np\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\log n}+\tau\log^{2}n\right)\big\|\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\big\|_{2,\infty}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. Here, the last inequality
arises from
\|\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq n\|\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}
.
Consequently, we conclude that, with high probability,

\left\|\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\left(\bm{A}_{1}+\bm{E}\right)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\left(\tau\sqrt{np\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\log n}+\tau\log^{2}n\right)\big\|\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\big\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{np\log n}\left\|\bm{Y}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty},

with the proviso that
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\ll 1/\log n

and
n^{2}p\gg n\log^{4}n
.∎

Proof of Fact
6
.

Regarding the second
term on the right-hand side of (
F.17
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{l,\cdot}\left(\bm{A}_{2}^{t}\right)\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\bm{R}^{t,(l)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{j=1}^{n}\left(\bm{A}_{2}^{t}\right)_{lj}\bm{Y}_{j,\cdot}^{t,(l)}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\leq}2np\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{\infty}\big\|\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\big\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(ii)}}{\leq}12np\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}C_{\infty}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\frac{\mu r}{n}\sigma_{\max}\left\|\bm{Y}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(iii)}}{\lesssim}\sigma\sqrt{np\log n}\left\|\bm{Y}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}.

Here, the first upper bound (i) arises from the fact that
\{j\mid(\bm{A}_{2}^{t})_{lj}\neq 0\}\subseteq\{j\mid(l,j)\in\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}\}
,
whose cardinality is upper bounded by
2np\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}
with
high probability as long as
np\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\gg\log n
. The
second inequality (ii) comes from the simple fact that
\|\bm{Y}^{t,(l)}\|_{2,\infty}\leq 2\|\bm{Y}^{\star}\|_{2,\infty}

as well as the bound

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{A}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}-\bm{X}^{t}\bm{Y}^{t\top}+\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}\right)\right]-\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}\right)\right]\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}-\bm{X}^{t}\bm{Y}^{t\top}+\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}\right)-\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{S}^{\star}+\bm{E}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\bm{Y}^{\star\top}-\bm{X}^{t}\bm{Y}^{t\top}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 3C_{\infty}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\frac{\mu r}{n}\sigma_{\max},

where we use the non-expansiveness of
\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(\cdot)

and the established bound (
F.15
), which holds as long as
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\ll 1/\kappa
.
Last but not least, the relation (iii) holds as long as
\rho_{\mathsf{s}}\leq\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\ll 1/(\kappa\mu r)

and
n\gg\kappa\mu r
.∎

F.6
Proof of Lemma
19

Without loss of generality, we assume
1\leq l\leq n
. Following
the definitions of
\bm{S}^{t+1,(l)}
and
\bm{S}^{t+1}
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}_{-l,\cdot}\big(\bm{S}^{t+1,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t+1}\big)\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathcal{P}_{-l,\cdot}\left[\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\big(\bm{M}-\bm{X}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)\top}\big)-\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\big(\bm{M}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top}\big)\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

(F.20)

where we denote
\bm{\Delta}\coloneqq\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(\bm{M}-\bm{X}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)\top})-\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(\bm{M}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top})
.
Recall from Appendix
A
that
each
(i,j)
is included in
\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}
independently
with probability
p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}
, where
1\geq\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\geq\rho_{\mathsf{s}}
.

1.

For the first term
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}\right)\|_{\mathrm{F}}
,
the non-expansiveness of the proximal operator
\mathcal{S}_{\tau}(\cdot)

yields

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\big(\bm{X}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)\top}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top}\big)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

Apply Lemma
4
and a similar
argument in bounding (
F.10
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left[\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\left(\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{R}^{t+1,(l)}-\bm{Y}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\right)^{\top}\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left[\left(\bm{X}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{R}^{t+1,(l)}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\right)\bm{R}^{t+1,(l)\top}\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)\top}\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left\|\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\right\|\left\|\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{R}^{t+1,(l)}-\bm{Y}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\kappa p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left\|\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{H}^{t+1,(l)}\right\|\left\|\bm{X}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{R}^{t+1,(l)}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

with the proviso that
n^{2}p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\gg\mu rn\log n
.
In view of (
F.6a
) and the simple facts

\|\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{H}^{t+1}\|\leq 2\|\bm{X}^{\star}\|,\|\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{H}^{t+1,(l)}\|\leq 2\|\bm{X}^{\star}\|
,
one has

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa p\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left\|\bm{X}^{\star}\right\|\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}+\frac{\lambda}{p\sigma_{\min}}\right)\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{n\log n}\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|,

provided that
\rho_{\mathsf{aug}}\ll 1/\kappa
.

2.

Regarding the second term
\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}\right)\|_{\mathrm{F}}
,
we first recall from (
F.16
) that

\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{M}-\bm{X}^{t+1}\bm{Y}^{t+1\top}\right)\right]=\bm{0}.

By replacing
\bm{X}^{t+1}
(resp.
\bm{Y}^{t+1})
with
\bm{X}^{t+1,(l)}
(resp.
\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)})
) and invoking (
F.19
) instead of (
F.3c
), the same arguments that we used to prove (
F.16
) also allow us to demonstrate

\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\left[\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{M}-\bm{X}^{t+1,(l)}\bm{Y}^{t+1,(l)\top}\right)\right]=\bm{0}

provided that
n^{2}p\gg\kappa^{4}\mu^{2}r^{2}n\log n
and
\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{p}}\ll 1/\kappa
. Consequently,
we have
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}^{\mathrm{c}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}\right)=\bm{0}
.

Substituting the above two bounds into (
F.20
),
we conclude that

\left\|\mathcal{P}_{-l,\cdot}\big(\bm{S}^{t+1,(l)}-\bm{S}^{t+1}\big)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{\mathsf{aug}}}\left(\bm{\Delta}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C_{3}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{n\log n}\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\bm{F}^{\star}\right\|.

F.7
Proof of Lemma
20

Following
[
CCF+20
, Lemma 18]
, we already know that

f\left(\bm{X}^{t+1},\bm{Y}^{t+1};\bm{S}^{t}\right)\leq f\left(\bm{X}^{t},\bm{Y}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)-\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|\nabla f\left(\bm{X}^{t},\bm{Y}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(F.21)

As a result, one has

\displaystyle F\left(\bm{X}^{t+1},\bm{Y}^{t+1},\bm{S}^{t+1}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\leq}F\left(\bm{X}^{t+1},\bm{Y}^{t+1},\bm{S}^{t}\right)=f\left(\bm{X}^{t+1},\bm{Y}^{t+1};\bm{S}^{t}\right)+\tau\left\|\bm{S}^{t}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}f\left(\bm{X}^{t},\bm{Y}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)-\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|\nabla f\left(\bm{X}^{t},\bm{Y}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\tau\left\|\bm{S}^{t}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle=F\left(\bm{X}^{t},\bm{Y}^{t},\bm{S}^{t}\right)-\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|\nabla f\left(\bm{X}^{t},\bm{Y}^{t};\bm{S}^{t}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

where (i) follows since, by construction,
\bm{S}^{t+1}
is the minimizer
of
F(\bm{X}^{t+1},\bm{Y}^{t+1},\bm{S})
for any given
(\bm{X}^{t+1},\bm{Y}^{t+1})
,
and (ii) arises from (
F.21
).
